79.11673pt\raise-16.72862pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.3491pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eta_{I\subset I^{\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 79.11673pt\raise-24.46724pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-26.35725pt\raise-33.45726pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{\mu Sh}^{\diamondsuit}_{\Lambda_{I}}(\Omega_{I})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.27567pt\raise-26.09615pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\eta}_{I}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.76701pt\raise-33.45726pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 55.76701pt\raise-33.45726pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Sh}^{\diamondsuit}_{\Lambda_{I}}(T^{I})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

commute.
Note that
the hyperbolic restriction in the coordinate direction indexed by

j\in I^{c}

vanishes on sheaves
whose microsupport does not intersect the locus

\{\xi_{j}>0\}\subset T^{*}T^{d+1}
.
For each

I\in(\mathcal{I}^{\circ}_{d+1})^{op}

the functor

\tilde{\eta}_{I}

is an equivalence,
since it admits an inverse induced by the pushforward

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sh}^{\diamondsuit}_{\Lambda_{I}}(T^{I})\to\operatorname{Sh}^{\diamondsuit}_{\Lambda_{d+1}}(T^{d+1})

along the inclusion

T^{I}\hookrightarrow T^{d+1}
.
∎

Corollary 2.31

(
[
Nad
, Corollary 5.28]
)
.

There is an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(\tilde{P}_{d})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\simeq\operatorname{\mu Sh}^{\diamondsuit}_{\Lambda_{d+1}}(\Omega_{d+1})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\simeq\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(\mathbb{A}^{d+2},y_{1}\cdots y_{d+2})

where

\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(\mathbb{A}^{d+2},y_{1}\cdots y_{d+2})

is the ind-completion of

\operatorname{MF}(\mathbb{A}^{d+2},y_{1}\cdots y_{d+2})
.

Proof.

The first equivalence follows from
Lemma
2.21
.
Taking
compact objects of the equivalence from
Theorem
2.30

and
passing to
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-folding,
one obtains the second equivalence from
Lemma
2.28
.
∎

3.
Critical loci of Landau–Ginzburg models for very affine hypersufaces

In this section,
following

[
AAK16
, Section 3]
,
we realize the mirror pair for very affine hypersuface as critical loci of associated Landau–Ginzburg models.
They give rise to fibrations over the tropical hypersurface equipped with the canonical stratification.

3.1.
Very affine hypersurfaces

Let

\mathbb{T}=M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}=M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}/M^{\vee}

be a real
(d+1)
-dimensional torus with cocharacter lattice

M^{\vee}
.
We denote by

\mathbb{T}_{\mathbb{C}}=M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}

the associated complex torus.
Taking its dual,
one obtains the complex torus

\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}=M_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}

associated with

\mathbb{T}^{\vee}=M_{\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}=M_{\mathbb{R}}/M

whose cocharacter lattice is

M
.
We choose an inner product to identify

T\mathbb{T}^{\vee}

with

T^{*}\mathbb{T}^{\vee}

and
regard

\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\cong T\mathbb{T}^{\vee}\cong T^{*}\mathbb{T}^{\vee}

as an exact symplectic manifold
equipped with the standard Liouville structure.

Definition 3.1
.

Let

\mathcal{T}

be a triangulation of a lattice polytope

\Delta^{\vee}\subset M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
We call

\mathcal{T}

adapted

if there is a convex piecewise function

\rho\colon\Delta^{\vee}\to\mathbb{R}

whose corner locus is

\mathcal{T}
.
We call

\mathcal{T}

unimodular

if each cell is congruent to the standard
(d+1)
-simplex

\Delta_{d+1}

under the
\operatorname{GL}(d+1,\mathbb{Z})
-action.

For a latiice polytope

\Delta^{\vee}\subset M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}
,
choose an adapted unimodular triangulation

\mathcal{T}
.
We denote by

A

the set of vertices of

\mathcal{T}
.
In other words,

\mathcal{T}

is the convex hull

\operatorname{Conv}(A)

of

A
.
The convex piecewise function

\rho\colon\Delta^{\vee}\to\mathbb{R}

defines a Laurent polynomial

(3.1)

\displaystyle W_{t}\colon\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\to\mathbb{C},\ x\mapsto\sum_{\alpha\in A}c_{\alpha}t^{-\rho(\alpha)}x^{\alpha}

in coordinates

x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d+1})

on

\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}
,
where

c_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{C}^{*}

are arbitrary constants
and

t\gg 0

is a tropicalization parameter.

Definition 3.2
.

For sufficiently general

t\gg 0

we call the hypersurface

H_{t}=W^{-1}_{t}(0)

very affine
.

Since

t

is sufficiently general,
a very affine hypersurface

H_{t}

is smooth.
Due to the above choice of inner product,
we may regard

H_{t}

as a Liouville submanifold of

\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Definition 3.3
.

The

amoeba

\Pi_{t}

of

H_{t}

is its image under

\operatorname{Log}_{d+1}\colon\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
.

Definition 3.4
.

The

tropical hypersurface

\Pi_{\Sigma}

associated with

H_{t}

is the hypersurface defined by the

tropical polynomial

\displaystyle\varphi\colon M_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathbb{R},\ \varphi(m)=\max\{\langle m,n\rangle-\rho(n)\ |\ n\in\Delta^{\vee}\}.

Namely,

\Pi_{\Sigma}

is the set of points
where
the maximum is achieved more than once.

According to

[
Mik04
, Corollary 6.4]
,
when

t\to\infty

the rescaled amoeba

\Pi_{t}/\log t

converges to

\Pi_{\Sigma}
.
It is known that

\Pi_{\Sigma}

is a deformation retract of

\Pi_{t}

for

t\gg 0
.
Combinatorially,

\Pi_{\Sigma}

is the dual cell complex of

\mathcal{T}
.
In particular,
the set of connected components of

\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\setminus\Pi_{\Sigma}

bijectively corresponds to

A

according to which

\alpha\in A

achieves the maximum of

\langle m,\alpha\rangle-\rho(\alpha)

for

m\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\setminus\Pi_{\Sigma}
.
Note that

\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\setminus\Pi_{t}

for

t\gg 0

has the same combinatrics as

\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\setminus\Pi_{\Sigma}
.

Remark 3.5
.

Each connected component of

\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\setminus\Pi_{\Sigma}

is the locus
where the monomial

c_{\alpha}t^{-\rho(\alpha)}x^{\alpha}

becomes dominant.

In the sequel,
we will fix a general

t\gg 0

and
drop

t

from the notation.

3.2.
Landau–Ginzburg
A
-models for very affine hypersurfaces

For

X=\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\times\mathbb{C}

with coordinates

(x,u)=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d+1},u)
,
consider a Laurent polynomial

\displaystyle W_{X}\colon X\to\mathbb{C},\ (x,u)\mapsto uW(x)

where

W

is the Laurent polynomial
(
3.1
).

Definition 3.6
.

Let

H\subset\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}

be a very affine hypersurface defined by the Laurent polynomial

W

from
(
3.1
).
We call the pair

(X,W_{X})

the

Landau–Ginzburg
A
-model

for

H
.

Definition 3.7
.

The

Newton polytope

\Delta^{\vee}_{X}

of

W_{X}

is the convex hull

\displaystyle\operatorname{Conv}(0,-\Delta^{\vee}\times\{1\})\subset M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}.

Remark 3.8
.

The polytope

\Delta^{\vee}_{X}

admits an adapted unimodular star-shaped triangulation

\tilde{\mathcal{T}}

canonically induced by

\mathcal{T}
.
Recall that
a triangulation of

\Delta^{\vee}_{X}

is

star-shaped

if all of its simplices not contained in the boundary

\partial\Delta^{\vee}_{X}

share a common vertex

0

[
GS1
, Definition 3.3.1]
.

Lemma 3.9
.

The critical locus

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{X})

is given by

\{u=0\}\cap\{W=0\}\subset X
.

Proof.

Express the tangent map

dW_{X}

of

W_{X}

as a vector

(udW,W)
.
Since

H\subset\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}

is smooth,

dW

nowhere vanishes.
Hence

\operatorname{rank}(dW_{X})=0

if and only if

u=0

and

W=0
.
∎

Remark 3.10
.

By
Lemma
3.9

the projection

\operatorname{pr}_{1}\colon X=\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\times\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}

preserves

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{X})
.
Let

\operatorname{ret}\colon\Pi\to\Pi_{\Sigma}

be the continuous map induced by the retraction.
Then the composition

(3.2)

\displaystyle f\colon H\cong\operatorname{Crit}(W_{X})\hookrightarrow X\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Log}_{d+1}}\Pi\xrightarrow{\operatorname{ret}}\Pi_{\Sigma}

gives the fibration from

[
Mik04
, Theorem 1’]
.
Recall that

k
-th intersections of legs of a pair of pants has torus factor of dimension

k
.
Away from lower dimensional strata,
the fiber over a point in a
k
-stratum contains a real
k
-torus in the torus factor.

3.3.
Landau–Ginzburg
B
-models for very affine hypersurfaces

Let

Y

be the noncompact
(d+2)
-dimensional toric variety associated with the fan

\displaystyle\Sigma_{Y}=\operatorname{Cone}(-\mathcal{T}\times\{1\})\subset M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}.

The primitive ray generators of

\Sigma_{Y}

are the vectors of the form

(-\alpha,1)

with

\alpha\in A
.
Such vectors span a smooth cone of

\Sigma_{Y}

if and only if

\alpha

span a cell of

\mathcal{T}
.

Dually,

Y

is associated with the noncompact moment polytope

\displaystyle\Delta_{Y}=\{(m,u)\in M_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}\ |\ u\geq\varphi(m)\}.

The facets of

\Delta_{Y}

correspond to the maximal domains of linearity of

\varphi
.
Hence the irreducible toric divisors of

Y

bijectively correspond to the connected components of

\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\setminus\Pi_{\Sigma}
.
In particular,
the combinatrics of toric strata of

Y

can be read off

\Pi_{\Sigma}
.

Remark 3.11
.

The noncompact polytope

\Delta_{Y}

is homeomorphic to the image of

Y

under the composition

(3.3)

\displaystyle Y\to(Y)_{\geq 0}\to M_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}

of
the map induced by retraction to the nonnegative real points
with
the restriction of negated algebraic moment map.

Lemma 3.12
.

Let

q\colon M_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}\to M_{\mathbb{R}}

be the natural projection.
Then under

q

the union of facets of

\Delta_{Y}

homeomorphically maps to

M_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Moreover,
the union of codimension

2

faces of

\Delta_{Y}

homeomorphically maps to

\Pi_{\Sigma}
.

Proof.

By construction of

\Sigma_{Y}

under

q

each facet of

\Delta_{Y}

homeomorphically maps to the maximal domain of linearity of

\varphi

corresponding to the same

\alpha\in A
.
Any codimension

2

face of

\Delta_{Y}

can be obtained as the intersection of two distinct facets.
Hence

q

restricted to the union of codimension

2

faces of

\Delta_{Y}

gives an injection to

\Pi_{\Sigma}
.
This is also surjective,
as each full dimensional face of

\Pi_{\Sigma}

is adjacent to exactly two maximal domains of linearity.
∎

For each

\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{d+1})\in A

let

Y_{\alpha}=(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+1}\times\mathbb{C}

with coordinates

y_{\alpha}=(y_{\alpha,1},\ldots,y_{\alpha,d+1},v_{\alpha})
,
where

y_{\alpha,1},\ldots,y_{\alpha,d+1},v_{\alpha}

are the monomials with weights

\displaystyle\eta_{1}=(-1,0,\ldots,0,-\alpha_{1}),\ldots,\eta_{d+1}=(0,\ldots,0,-1,-\alpha_{d+1}),\eta_{d+2}=(0,\ldots,0,1)\in M\times\mathbb{Z}.

Their pairing with the monomial with weight

(-\alpha,1)\in M^{\vee}\times\mathbb{Z}

yields

0,\ldots,0,1

respectively.

Lemma 3.13
.

The complex algebraic variety

Y_{\alpha}

is the affine open subset of

Y

associated with the ray spanned by

(-\alpha,1)\in M^{\vee}\times\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Suppose that

\sigma\in\Sigma_{Y}(1)

is the cone associated with the affine open subset

Y_{\alpha}\subset Y
.
We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}(y^{\pm 1}_{\alpha,i})=\sum_{\xi\in\sigma(1)}\langle\pm\eta_{i},u_{\xi}\rangle D_{\xi},\ \operatorname{div}(v_{\alpha})=\sum_{\xi\in\sigma(1)}\langle\eta_{d+2},u_{\xi}\rangle D_{\xi},

where

u_{\xi}

are primitive ray generators of

\xi

and

D_{\xi}=\overline{O(\xi)}

are the closures of the orbits corresponding to

\xi
.
Since

y^{\pm 1}_{\alpha,1},\ldots,y^{\pm 1}_{\alpha,d+1}

never vanish on

Y_{\alpha}
,
pairing of

\eta_{i}

with the primitive ray generators in

\sigma

must yield

0

for

1\leq i\leq d+1
.
On the other hand,
pairing of

\eta_{d+2}

with the primitive ray generators of

\sigma

must yield

1
.
∎

Due to the above lemma,

Y_{\alpha}

covers the open stratum of

Y

and
the open stratum of the irreducible toric divisor corresponding to

\alpha
.
If

\alpha,\beta\in A

are connected by an edge in

\mathcal{T}
,
then we glue

Y_{\alpha}

to

Y_{\beta}

with the coordinate transformations

\displaystyle y_{\alpha,i}=v^{\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i}}_{\beta}y_{\beta,i},\ v_{\alpha}=v_{\beta},\ 1\leq i\leq d+1.

Thus the coordinate charts

\{Y_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in A}

cover the complement in

Y

of the codimension more than

1

strata.

We may write

v

for

v_{\alpha}

as it does not depend on the choice of

\alpha\in A
.
Since the weight

(0,\ldots,0,1)

pairs nonnegatively with the primitive ray generators of

\Sigma_{Y}
,
the monomial

v

defines a regular function on

Y
,
which we denote by

W_{Y}
.

Definition 3.14
.

Let

H\subset\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}

be a very affine hypersurface defined by the Laurent polynomial

W

from
(
3.1
).
We call the pair

(Y,W_{Y})

the

Landau–Ginzburg
B
-model

for

H
.

Remark 3.15
.

The pair

(Y,W_{Y})

is a conjectural SYZ mirror to

H

[
AAK16
, Theorem 1.6]
.

The critical locus

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{Y})

is the preimage of the codimension

2

strata of

\Delta_{Y}

under
(
3.3
).
One can check this locally in each affine chart, which is isomorphic to

\mathbb{C}^{d+2}

as

\mathcal{T}

is unimodular.

Lemma 3.16
.

The critical locus

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{Y})

is given by

\bigcup_{\alpha\in A}\overline{Y}_{\alpha}\setminus Y_{\alpha}\subset Y
.

Proof.

For each

\alpha\in A

the intersection

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{Y})\cap Y_{\alpha}

is empty.
Indeed,
when restricted to

Y_{\alpha}
,
the tangent map

dW_{Y}

of

W_{Y}

is expressed as a vector
whose last factor is

1
.
Hence

dW_{Y}|_{Y_{\alpha}}

is surjective
and
we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{Crit}(W_{Y})\subset Y\setminus\bigcup_{\alpha\in A}Y_{\alpha}=\bigcup_{\alpha\in A}\overline{Y}_{\alpha}\setminus Y_{\alpha}.

Take any point

y\in\overline{Y}_{\alpha}\setminus Y_{\alpha}
.
Suppose that
there is a vertex

\alpha^{\prime}\in A

connected with

\alpha

by an edge in

\mathcal{T}

such that

y\in\overline{Y}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\setminus Y_{\alpha^{\prime}}
.
Let

\sigma\in\Sigma_{Y}

be the cone generated by two rays

\xi_{\alpha}=\operatorname{Cone}(-\alpha,1),\xi_{\alpha^{\prime}}=\operatorname{Cone}(-\alpha^{\prime},1)
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}(v)=\langle\eta_{d+2},u_{\xi_{\alpha}}\rangle D_{\xi_{\alpha}}+\langle\eta_{d+2},u_{\xi_{\alpha^{\prime}}}\rangle D_{\xi_{\alpha^{\prime}}}

on the associated affine open subset

\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\sigma^{\vee}\cap(M\times\mathbb{Z})]\cong(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}

of

Y
.
Hence the restriction of

dW_{Y}

vanishes on

D_{\xi_{\alpha}}\cap D_{\xi^{\prime}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
,
which is

\displaystyle((\overline{Y}_{\alpha}\setminus Y_{\alpha})\cap(\overline{Y}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\setminus Y_{\alpha^{\prime}}))|_{\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\sigma^{\vee}\cap(M\times\mathbb{Z})]}.

The union of such intersections for all

\alpha^{\prime}\in A

is

\overline{Y}_{\alpha}\setminus Y_{\alpha}
.
Applying the same argument to the other cases,
we obtain

\displaystyle\bigcup_{\alpha\in A}\overline{Y}_{\alpha}\setminus Y_{\alpha}\subset\operatorname{Crit}(W_{Y}).

∎

Remark 3.17
.

Since the map
(
3.3
)
sends each
k
-th intersection of

D_{\xi_{\alpha}},\alpha\in A

to a codimension

k

face of

\Delta_{Y}
,
by
Lemma
3.16

it sends

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{Y})

to the union of codimension

2

faces.
On the other hand,
by
Lemma
3.12

the map

q\colon M_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}\to M_{\mathbb{R}}

homeomorphically sends the union of codimension

2

faces of

\Delta_{Y}

to

\Pi_{\Sigma}
.
Hence the composition

(3.4)

\displaystyle g\colon\operatorname{Crit}(W_{Y})\hookrightarrow Y\xrightarrow{\textup{(\ref{eq:moment})}}\Delta_{Y}\xrightarrow{q}\Pi_{\Sigma}

gives a fibration.
The fiber over a point in a
k
-stratum is a real
k
-torus

[
CLS11
, Prop 12.2.3(b)]
.

4.
Constructible sheaves of categories

In this section,
we define two constructible sheaves of categories over the tropical hypersurface

\Pi_{\Sigma}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d+1}

with the canonical stratification
and
a certain topology generated by the vertices.
In the sequel,
by a

Liouville manifold

we mean a Liouville manifold of finite type,
i.e.,
the completion of some Liouville domain.
By a

Weinstein manifold

we mean a Liouville manifold together with a Morse–Bott function constant on the cylindrical ends for
which the Liouville vector field is gradient-like.

Definition 4.1
.

We introduce into

\Pi_{\Sigma}

with the canonical stratification a topology defined as follows.
To a vertex

v\in\Pi_{\Sigma}

we define the associated open subset

U_{v}

as the union of all strata adjacent to

v
,
which is homeomorphic to a
d
-dimensional tropical pants.
To an edge

e\subset\Pi_{\Sigma}

connecting two vertices

v_{1},v_{2}

we define the associated open subset

U_{e}

as the intersection

U_{v_{1}}\cap U_{v_{2}}
.
Similarly,
to each
k
-stratum

S^{(k)}\subset\Pi_{\Sigma}

adjacent to

l

vertices

v_{1},\ldots,v_{l}

we define the associated open subset

U_{S^{(k)}}

as the intersection

U_{v_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap U_{v_{l}}
.
A general open subset

U

is of the form

U_{S^{(k_{1})}}\cup\cdots\cup U_{S^{(k_{m})}}

for some strata

S^{(k_{1})},\ldots,S^{(k_{m})}\subset\Pi_{\Sigma}
.

4.1.
A
-side partially presheaves of categories for very affine hypersurfaces

Fix a pants decomposition of

H\subset\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\cong T^{*}T^{d+1}

[
Mik04
, Theorem 1’]
.

Definition 4.2
.

The

A
-side partially presheaf

\mathcal{F}^{pre}_{A}

of categories for

H

is a collection

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{F}^{pre}_{A}(U_{S^{(k)}}),R^{A}_{S^{(k)},S^{(l)}}\}

of
sections
and
restriction functors
defined on connected open subsets of

\Pi_{\Sigma}

as follows:

•

The section over

U_{S^{(k)}}

is given by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-folding of the ind-completion of the wrapped Fukaya category

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{pre}_{A}(U_{S^{(k)}})=\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{S^{(k)}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}

where

H_{S^{(k)}}

is the inverse image of suitably shrunk

U_{S^{(k)}}

under
(
3.2
).
In other words,

H_{S^{(k)}}

is symplectomorphic to the intersection of

k

legs of

\tilde{P}_{d}
.

•

Along an inclusion

U_{S^{(l)}}\hookrightarrow U_{S^{(k)}}

the restriction functor is given by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-folding of the ind-completion of the Viterbo restriction

[
GPS2
, Section 11.1]

\displaystyle R^{A}_{S^{(k)},S^{(l)}}=(V^{A}_{S^{(k)},S^{(l)}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\colon\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{S^{(k)}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\to\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{S^{(l)}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}.

We will show that

\mathcal{F}^{pre}_{A}

is well defined.
First,
since our Liouville manifolds are of finite type,
the section is unique up to canonical equivalence.
In particular,
the sections are well defined.
The following is a special case of

[
GPS2
, Lemma 3.4]
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let

\lambda_{S^{(k)}},\lambda^{\prime}_{S^{(k)}}

be the completions of two Liouville forms on a Liouville domain

[H_{S^{(k)}}]

completing to

H_{S^{(k)}}
.
Then there is a canonical equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{S^{(k)}},\lambda_{U_{S}^{(k)}})=\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{S^{(k)}},\lambda^{\prime}_{U_{S}^{(k)}}).

Proof.

Our argument is essentially the same as

[
Jef22
, Lemma 2]
.
Since the space of Liouville forms for a compact symplectic manifold-with-boundary is convex,
any two Liouville forms on

[H_{S^{(k)}}]

are canonically homotopic
and
the homotopy completes to that for

\lambda_{S^{(k)}},\lambda^{\prime}_{S^{(k)}}
.
Then one can apply

[
CE12
, Proposition 11.8]

to obtain a strictly exact symplectomorphism

\psi\colon H_{S^{(k)}}\to H_{S^{(k)}}
.
By definition it satisfies

\psi^{*}\lambda^{\prime}-\lambda=df

for some compactly supported function

f\colon H_{S^{(k)}}\to\mathbb{R}
.
In particular,

\psi

defines a trivial inclusion of open Liouville sectors in the sense of

[
GPS2
, Definition 3.3]
.
Then one can apply

[
GPS2
, Lemma 3.4]

to see that
the pushforward functor from

[
GPS1
, Section 3.6]

gives the canonical equivalence.
∎

In the sequel,
we drop Liouville structures from the notation.
Since
by

[
Mik04
, Remark 5.2]

each piece of the pants decomposition can be made symplectomorphic to

\tilde{P}_{d}
,
we obtain

Corollary 4.4
.

For each vertex

v\in\Pi_{\Sigma}

the section

\mathcal{F}^{pre}_{A}(U_{v})

is given by

\operatorname{Fuk}(\tilde{P}_{d})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
.

Let

\tilde{P}_{S^{(k)}}

be the intersection of
k
-legs of

\tilde{P}_{d}

mapping onto

U_{S^{(k)}}

under

\operatorname{Log}_{d+1}
.

Corollary 4.5
.

For each

U_{S^{(k)}}=\cap^{l}_{i=1}U_{v_{i}}

the section

\mathcal{F}^{pre}_{A}(U_{S^{(k)}})

is given by

\operatorname{Fuk}(\tilde{P}_{S^{(k)}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
.

Next,
along an inclusion

U_{S^{(l)}}\hookrightarrow U_{S^{(k)}}

the restriction functor comes from a certain quotient functor.
In particular,
the restriction functors are well defined.

Lemma 4.6
.

Along an inclusion

U_{S^{(l)}}\hookrightarrow U_{S^{(k)}}

the Viterbo restriction functor is given by the quotient by the cocores of

\tilde{P}_{S^{(k)}}

not in

\tilde{P}_{S^{(l)}}
.
Here,
we regagrd

[\tilde{P}_{S^{(l)}}]

as a Weinstein subdomain of

\tilde{P}_{S^{(k)}}

with respect to Nadler’s Weinstein structure.

Proof.

As the other cases can be proved similarly,
we restrict ourselves to the case
where

S^{(k)}=U_{v}

and

S^{(l)}=U_{e}

for some edge

e

connecting

v

with

v^{\prime}
.
By Corollary

4.5

the section

\mathcal{F}^{pre}_{A}(U_{e})

is given by

\operatorname{Fuk}(\tilde{P}_{e})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
.
Permuting legs by
\mathfrak{S}_{d+2}
-action if necessary,
we may assume that

\tilde{P}_{e}

does not correspond to the final leg of

\tilde{P}_{v}
.
Since both

[\tilde{P}_{e}]

and
the cobordism

[\tilde{P}_{v}]\setminus[\tilde{P}_{e}]^{\circ}

are Weinstein,
one can apply

[
GPS2
, Proposition 11.2]

to see that
the Viterbo restriction coincides with the quotient by the cocores of

\tilde{P}_{v}

not in

\tilde{P}_{e}
.
∎

4.2.
A
-side constructible sheaves of categories for very affine hypersurfaces

Since

U_{v}

for all

v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Pi_{\Sigma})

form a subbase of the topology of

\Pi_{\Sigma}
,
we may pass to its sheafification.

Definition 4.7
.

The

A
-side constructible sheaf of categories

for

H

is the sheafification

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{A}\colon\operatorname{Open}(\Pi_{\Sigma})^{op}\to^{**}\operatorname{DG},

where

\operatorname{Open}(\Pi_{\Sigma})

is the category of open subsets of

\Pi_{\Sigma}

with respect to the topology defined in Definition

4.1
.

Remark 4.8
.

In general,
the existence of sheafification might be delicate because of size issues.
However,
this is not the case in our setting as

{}^{**}\operatorname{DG}

has
small limits
and
colimits,
and
the topology on

\Pi_{\Sigma}

has finite cardinality.

We will show that
the global section is given by the wrapped Fukaya category of

H
.
In our proof,
the following two lemmas play key roles.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let

\mathscr{C}

be a stable presentable dg category
and

\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B}

its full presentable dg subcategories
such that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathscr{C}}(A,B)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathscr{C}}(B,A)=0

for any

A\in\mathscr{A},B\in\mathscr{B}
.
Then there is a fiber product

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern Proof.

Since we have the pushouts

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 134.37506pt\raise-23.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.5pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{A},}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 84.2917pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 109.08337pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 120.33337pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 120.33337pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{B},}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

the Verdier localizations

\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{A},\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{B}

admit right adjoints as well as the inclusions

\mathscr{A}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C},\mathscr{B}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}
.
Hence we obtain two semiorthogonal decompositions of

\mathscr{C}

by

\mathscr{A},\mathscr{A}^{\perp}

and
by

\mathscr{B},\mathscr{B}^{\perp}
,
which respectively yield cofiber sequences

\displaystyle C_{\mathscr{A}}\to C\to C_{\mathscr{A}^{\perp}},\ C_{\mathscr{B}}\to C\to C_{\mathscr{B}^{\perp}},\ C_{\mathscr{A}\oplus\mathscr{B}}\to C\to C_{(\mathscr{A}\oplus\mathscr{B})^{\perp}}

for any object

C\in\mathscr{C}
.
Note that
the full dg subcategory

\langle\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B}\rangle\subset\mathscr{C}

is equivalent to

\mathscr{A}\oplus\mathscr{B}
,
as

\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B}

are mutually orthogonal.
Here,
the morphism

C_{\mathscr{A}\oplus\mathscr{B}}\to C

in the last cofiber sequence is the direct sum of that

C_{\mathscr{A}}\to C,C_{\mathscr{B}}\to C

in the first two.
Since

\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B}

are mutually orthogonal,
its cone can be computed by taking the cone of

C_{\mathscr{B}}\to C

followed by taking the cone of

C_{\mathscr{A}}\to C_{\mathscr{B}^{\perp}}
.
Hence we obtain a cofiber sequence

\displaystyle C_{\mathscr{A}}\to C_{\mathscr{B}^{\perp}}\to C_{(\mathscr{A}\oplus\mathscr{B})^{\perp}}.

The conclusion is equivalent to there being a fiber product

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 39.92506pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.98059pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise of morphism complexes for any

.
Note that
it suffices to check the latter
when

.
Since

are the images of

under the right adjoints of the Verdier localizations

,
one can rewrite
(

)
as

Now,
as the functor

preserves fiber products,
it suffices to show that

is a fiber product in

.

Since both
the left
and
the outer
squares are pushouts,
the right square is also a pushout.
It follows that
(
4.2
)
is a fiber product,
as for stable dg categories
any fiber product is a bicartesian.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Let

W

be a
d
-dimensional Weinstein manifold
with a fixed pants decomposition.
Consider the gluing

W\cup\tilde{P}_{d}

of Weinstein manifolds

W

with

\tilde{P}_{d}

along a union of

l

legs of

\tilde{P}_{d}

for

1\leq l<d+2

with restricted Nadler’s Weinstein structures.
Then we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Fuk}(W\cup\tilde{P}_{d})}(L_{1},L_{2})=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Fuk}(W\cup\tilde{P}_{d})}(L_{2},L_{1})=0

for cocores

L_{1},L_{2}

of

W\cup\tilde{P}_{d}

respectively not in

\tilde{P}_{d},W
.

Proof.

As the other cases can be proved similarly,
we restrict ourselves to the simplest case.
Consider the gluing

\tilde{P}^{1}_{d}\cup_{\operatorname{Core}(C)}\tilde{P}^{2}_{d}

of two
d
-dimensional pairs of pants along a leg

C
,
where

\tilde{P}^{1}_{d},\tilde{P}^{2}_{d}

are equipped with Nadler’s Weinstein structures.
Here,
we choose their final legs different from

C
.
Then

[\tilde{P}^{1}_{d}],[\tilde{P}^{2}_{d}]

are Weinstein subdomains of

[\tilde{P}^{1}_{d}\cup_{\operatorname{Core}(C)}\tilde{P}^{2}_{d}]
.
Since the cobordisms

[\tilde{P}^{1}_{d}\cup_{\operatorname{Core}(C)}\tilde{P}^{2}_{d}]\setminus[\tilde{P}^{1}_{d}]^{\circ},\ [\tilde{P}^{1}_{d}\cup_{\operatorname{Core}(C)}\tilde{P}^{2}_{d}]\setminus[\tilde{P}^{2}_{d}]^{\circ}

are also Weinstein,
by

[
GPS2
, Proposition 11.2]

the Viterbo restriction functors

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}^{\omega}(\tilde{P}^{1}_{d}\cup_{\operatorname{Core}(C)}\tilde{P}^{2}_{d})\to\operatorname{Fuk}^{\omega}(\tilde{P}^{1}_{d}),\ \operatorname{Fuk}^{\omega}(\tilde{P}^{1}_{d}\cup_{\operatorname{Core}(C)}\tilde{P}^{2}_{d})\to\operatorname{Fuk}^{\omega}(\tilde{P}^{2}_{d})

are the quotients by the cocores respectively not in

\tilde{P}^{1}_{d},\tilde{P}^{2}_{d}
.
Here,

(-)^{\omega}

denotes taking compact objects.

Let

L_{1},L_{2}

be cocores of

\tilde{P}^{1}_{d}\cup_{\operatorname{Core}(C)}\tilde{P}^{2}_{d}

respectively not in

\tilde{P}^{2}_{d},\tilde{P}^{1}_{d}
.
We claim that the generating set of Floer complex

CF^{\bullet}(L_{1},L_{2})

is empty.
Suppose that the time
1
trajectory

\phi^{1}_{\operatorname{Ham}}(L_{1})

of

L_{1}

under the Hamiltonian flow

\phi_{\operatorname{Ham}}

intersects

L_{2}
.
Then one finds a point

p\in L_{1}

which needs to be pushed from the initial position through

C

to reach

L_{2}
.
Let

0<t<1

be the minimum time
such that

\phi^{t}_{\operatorname{Ham}}(p)\in\partial\bar{C}

and

\phi^{t+\epsilon}_{\operatorname{Ham}}(p)\in C\setminus\partial\bar{C}

for

0<\epsilon\ll 1
.
By construction the Hamiltonian flow on the gluing region is orthogonal to the Liouville flow on

C
.
Recall that
on cylindrical ends the wrapping is defined by the Hamiltonian flow
which is orthogonal to the Liouville flow
and
we glue

\tilde{P}^{1}_{d},\tilde{P}^{2}_{d}

along their skeleta inside

C
.
Consider its restriction to the first factors in the product decompositions

\displaystyle C\cong\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z}\times\tilde{P}_{d-1},\ C\cong\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\bar{z}}\times\tilde{P}_{d-1}

of

C

respectively as a leg of

\tilde{P}^{1}_{d},\tilde{P}^{2}_{d}
.
The restricted Liouville flow to

\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z},\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\bar{z}}

is parallel to their radial coordinate directions.
Hence

\phi^{t+\epsilon}_{\operatorname{Ham}}(p)

would never exit

C

to reach

L_{2}
,
leading to contradiction.
∎

Theorem 4.11
.

The canonical functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(H)_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\to\mathcal{F}_{A}(\Pi_{\Sigma})=\lim\left(\prod_{v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Pi_{\Sigma})}\mathcal{F}_{A}(U_{v})\to\prod_{e\in\operatorname{Edge}(\Pi_{\Sigma})}\mathcal{F}_{A}(U_{e})\to\cdots\right)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

We begin with a vertex

v^{0}_{1}\in\Pi_{\Sigma}

to which some free edge is adjacent.
Here,
by a free edge we mean an edge not connecting two vertices.
There are in total

l_{1}<d+2

vertices

v^{1}_{1},\ldots,v^{1}_{l_{1}}\in\Pi_{\Sigma}

connected with

v^{0}_{1}

by single edges

e^{1}_{11},\ldots,e^{1}_{1l_{1}}
.
Let

v^{k}_{1},\ldots,v^{k}_{l_{k}}

be the vertices of

\Pi_{\Sigma}

connected with

v^{0}_{1}

by at least

k

edges.
Each

v^{k}_{i}

is connected with at least one vertex

v^{k-1}_{j}

by a single edge

e^{k}_{ji}

for some

1\leq j\leq l_{k-1}
.
We will compute the section of

\mathcal{F}_{A}

over the union

\displaystyle U_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i_{1}=1}U_{v^{1}_{i_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\bigcup^{l_{k}}_{i_{k}=1}U_{v^{k}_{i_{k}}}.

Consider Nadler’s Weinstein structures transported to

H_{v^{0}_{1}},H_{v^{1}_{1}}

whose final legs correspond to different edges from

e^{1}_{11}
.
Then

H_{v^{0}_{1}},H_{v^{1}_{1}}

glue along their Weinstein submanifold

H_{e^{1}_{11}}

to yield
a Weinstein manifold

H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup H_{v^{1}_{1}}
.
Note that
the pants decomposition from

[
Mik04
, Theorem 1’]

is nothing but the gluing of the closures of tailored pants along their boundaries.
By

[
Mik04
, Remark 5.2]

this gluing is compatible with the natural symplectic structures.
To the product of their boundaries with a sufficiently small open interval,
one can transport Nadler’s Weinstein structure restricted to

H_{e^{1}_{11}}

via radial deformation.
By
Lemma
4.9

and
Lemma
4.10

we obtain a canonical equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup H_{v^{1}_{1}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}=\mathcal{F}_{A}(U_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup U_{v^{1}_{1}}).

Consider Nadler’s Weinstein structures transported to

H_{v^{0}_{1}},H_{v^{1}_{1}},H_{v^{1}_{2}}

whose final legs correspond to different edges from

e^{1}_{11},e^{1}_{12}

and
possibly existing edge

e^{11}_{12}

connecting

v^{1}_{1}

with

v^{1}_{2}
.
Note that
each of

H_{v^{0}_{1}},H_{v^{1}_{1}},H_{v^{1}_{2}}

has at least one free leg
which is not involved in this gluing.
Then

H_{v^{0}_{1}},H_{v^{1}_{1}},H_{v^{1}_{2}}

glue along their Weinstein submanifolds

H_{e^{1}_{11}},H_{e^{1}_{12}},H_{e^{11}_{12}}

to yield a Weinstein manifold

H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup H_{v^{1}_{1}}\cup H_{v^{1}_{2}}
.
By
Lemma
4.9

and
Lemma
4.10

we obtain a canonical equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup H_{v^{1}_{1}}\cup H_{v^{1}_{2}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}=\mathcal{F}_{A}(U_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup U_{v^{1}_{1}}\cup U_{v^{1}_{2}}).

Iteratively,
we obtain a canonical equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i=1}H_{v^{1}_{i}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}=\mathcal{F}_{A}(U_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i=1}U_{v^{1}_{i}}).

Suppose that
the canonical functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i_{1}=1}H_{v^{1}_{i_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\bigcup^{l_{k-1}}_{i_{k-1}=1}H_{v^{k-1}_{i_{k-1}}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\to\mathcal{F}_{A}(U_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i_{1}=1}U_{v^{1}_{i_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\bigcup^{l_{k-1}}_{i_{k-1}=1}U_{v^{k-1}_{i_{k-1}}})

is an equivalence.
When

v^{k}_{i}

is connected with only one vertex

v^{k-1}_{j}
,
consider Nadler’s Weinstein structures transported to

H_{v^{k-1}_{j}},H_{v^{k}_{i}}

whose final legs correspond to different edges from

e^{k}_{ji}
.
The former extends to give another Weinstein structure on

H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i_{1}=1}H_{v^{1}_{i_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\bigcup^{l_{k-1}}_{i_{k-1}=1}H_{v^{k-1}_{i_{k-1}}}

as follows.
First,
recall the translated Liouville structure

\alpha^{-l}_{d+1}

on

T^{d+1}_{\mathbb{C}}

from
(
2.1
).
Note that
we negate

l

here.
Restrict it to

\tilde{P}_{d}

and
then transport to all the other legs but the final compatibly with the outward Liouville vector field from

H_{v^{k-1}_{j}}
.
To the final leg,
transport its slight modification.
Namely,
push the zero locus of the Liouville vector field associated with

\alpha^{-l}_{d+1}

far away along the positive diagonal direction.
Then the closure of

\tilde{P}_{d}

becomes a Weinstein cobordisim.
We extend this along the direction towards cylindrical ends of

H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i_{1}=1}H_{v^{1}_{i_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\bigcup^{l_{k-1}}_{i_{k-1}=1}H_{v^{k-1}_{i_{k-1}}}
.
Note that
the extension might not be canonical.

In any case,
the result is
a Liouville manifold of finite type
and
Liouville homotopic to the standard Liouville structure,
which can be canonically upgraded to Weinstein.
Then

H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i_{1}=1}H_{v^{1}_{i_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\bigcup^{l_{k-1}}_{i_{k-1}=1}H_{v^{k-1}_{i_{k-1}}},H_{v^{k}_{i}}

glue along their Weinstein submanifold

H_{e^{k}_{ji}}

to yield a Weinstein manifold

H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i_{1}=1}H_{v^{1}_{i_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\bigcup^{l_{k-1}}_{i_{k-1}=1}H_{v^{k-1}_{i_{k-1}}}\cup H_{v^{k}_{i}}
.
Here,
we use the same symbol to denote the exact symplectic manifolds with modified Weinstein structure.
By
Lemma
4.9

and
Lemma
4.10

we obtain a canonical equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i_{1}=1}H_{v^{1}_{i_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\bigcup^{l_{k-1}}_{i_{k-1}=1}H_{v^{k-1}_{i_{k-1}}}\cup H_{v^{k}_{i}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}=\mathcal{F}_{A}(U_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i_{1}=1}U_{v^{1}_{i_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\bigcup^{l_{k-1}}_{i_{k-1}=1}U_{v^{k-1}_{i_{k-1}}}\cup U_{v^{k}_{i}}).

When

v^{k}_{i}

is connected with more than one vertices

v^{k-1}_{j_{1}},\ldots,v^{k-1}_{j_{l}}

by single edges,
consider Nadler’s Weinstein structures transported to

H_{v^{k-1}_{j_{1}}},\ldots,H_{v^{k-1}_{j_{l}}},H_{v^{k}_{i}}

whose final legs correspond to different edges from the ones connecting any two of

v^{k-1}_{j_{1}},\ldots,v^{k-1}_{j_{l}},v^{k}_{i}
.
The Weinstein structure on

H_{v^{k-1}_{j_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup H_{v^{k-1}_{j_{l}}}

extends to give another Weinstein structure on

H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i_{1}=1}H_{v^{1}_{i_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\bigcup^{l_{k-1}}_{i_{k-1}=1}H_{v^{k-1}_{i_{k-1}}}

in a similar way as above.
Iteratively,
we obtain a canonical equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i_{1}=1}H_{v^{1}_{i_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\bigcup^{l_{k}}_{i_{k}=1}H_{v^{k}_{i_{k}}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}=\mathcal{F}_{A}(U_{v^{0}_{1}}\cup\bigcup^{l_{1}}_{i_{1}=1}U_{v^{1}_{i_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\bigcup^{l_{k}}_{i_{k}=1}U_{v^{k}_{i_{k}}}).

∎

4.3.
B
-side constructible sheaves of categories for very affine hypersurfaces

Recall that

\Pi_{\Sigma}

is the dual cell complex of

\mathcal{T}
,
which we assume to be an adapted unimodular triangulation of the convex lattice polytope

\Delta^{\vee}\subset M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
In particular,
each vertex

v\in\Pi_{\Sigma}

bijectively corresponds to a cell congruent to a standard simplex under the
\operatorname{GL}(d+1,\mathbb{Z})
-action.
The cell in turn bijectively corresponds to a cone

\sigma_{v}\in\Sigma_{Y}
,
which defines an affine open subvariety

Y_{v}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\sigma^{\vee}_{v}\cap(M\times\mathbb{Z})]\subset Y

isomorphic to

\mathbb{A}^{d+2}
.
Introduce coordinates

(y_{e^{v}_{1}},\ldots,y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})

on

Y_{v}
,
where

e^{v}_{i}

stand for edges adjacent to

v

and
dual to facets

\sigma_{e^{v}_{i}}

of

\sigma_{v}

so that

Y_{e^{v}_{i}}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\sigma^{\vee}_{e^{v}_{i}}\cap(M\times\mathbb{Z})]\subset Y_{v}

are the open subvarieties defined by

y_{e^{v}_{i}}\neq 0
.

Definition 4.12
.

The

B
-side constructible sheaf

\mathcal{F}_{B}

of categories for

H

is a collection

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{F}_{B}(U_{S^{(k)}}),R^{B}_{S^{(k)},S^{(l)}}\}

of
sections
and
restriction functors
defined on connected open subsets of

\Pi_{\Sigma}

as follows:

•

The section over

U_{S^{(k)}}

is given by the ind-completion of the category of matrix factorizations

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{B}(U_{S^{(k)}})=\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{v}|_{\bigcap^{k}_{m=1}\{y_{e^{v}_{i_{m}}}\neq 0\}},y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}}),

where

e^{v}_{i_{1}},\ldots,e^{v}_{i_{k}}

are the edges adjacent to

v

determining the
k
-stratum

S^{(k)}
.

•

Along an inclusion

U_{S^{(l)}}\hookrightarrow U_{S^{(k)}}

the restriction functor is given by the canonical restriction functor

\displaystyle R^{B}_{S^{(k)},S^{(l)}}\colon\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{v}|_{\bigcap^{k}_{m=1}\{y_{e^{v}_{i_{m}}}\neq 0\}},y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})\to\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{v}|_{\bigcap^{l}_{n=1}\{y_{e^{v}_{j_{n}}}\neq 0\}},y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}}),

where

e^{v}_{i_{1}},\ldots,e^{v}_{i_{k}}

and

e^{v}_{j_{1}},\ldots,e^{v}_{j_{l}}

are edges adjacent to

v

respectively determining

S^{(k)}

and

S^{(l)}
.

Note that

\mathcal{F}_{B}

defines a sheaf on

\Pi_{\Sigma}

without passing to sheafification,
since

U_{v}

for all

v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Pi_{\Sigma})

form a subbase of the topology of

\Pi_{\Sigma}

and

\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}

is a sheaf on

Y

[
Pre
, Proposition A.3.1]
.

Lemma 4.13
.

There is a canonical equivalence

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Pi_{\Sigma})=\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y,W_{Y})

compatible with restrictions.
In particular,
for every open subset

U\subset\Pi_{\Sigma}

it makes the diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 77.13547pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{B}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.68878pt\raise-15.97223pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.68878pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-33.25317pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 42.25317pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 57.25317pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 57.25317pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{U},W_{Y})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

commute
where horizontal arrows are the restrictions.
Here,

Y_{U}\subset Y

is the open subset mapping to

U

under the composition of

(
3.3
)

with

q\colon M_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}\to M_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

Take an affine open cover

\bigcup_{v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Pi_{\Sigma})}Y_{v}

of

Y
.
By definition of

\mathcal{F}_{B}

we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Pi_{\Sigma})=\lim\left(\prod_{v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Pi_{\Sigma})}\mathcal{F}_{B}(U_{v})\rightarrow\prod_{e\in\operatorname{Edge}(\Pi_{\Sigma})}\mathcal{F}_{B}(U_{e})\rightarrow\cdots\right).

If

e

is an edge connecting two vertices

v,v^{\prime}
,
then the associated restriction functors are

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}R^{B}_{v,e}\colon\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{v},y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})\to\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{v}|_{\{y_{e^{v}_{i}}\neq 0\}},y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}}),\\
R^{B}_{v^{\prime},e}\colon\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{v^{\prime}},y_{e^{v^{\prime}}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v^{\prime}}_{d+2}})\to\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{v^{\prime}}|_{\{y_{e^{v^{\prime}}_{j}}\neq 0\}},y_{e^{v^{\prime}}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v^{\prime}}_{d+2}}).\end{gathered}

Recall that

\Pi_{\Sigma}

encodes all the combinatorial information to recover both

H

and

Y

from pieces.
In particular,
it gives a coordinate transformation

\displaystyle(y_{e^{v^{\prime}}_{1}},\cdots,y_{e^{v^{\prime}}_{d+2}})\to(y_{e^{v}_{1}},\cdots,y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})

on

Y_{e}

which defines a gluing datum

\displaystyle\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{v}|_{\{y_{e^{v}_{i}}\neq 0\}},y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})\simeq\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{v^{\prime}}|_{\{y_{e^{v^{\prime}}_{j}}\neq 0\}},y_{e^{v^{\prime}}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v^{\prime}}_{d+2}}).

As

Y_{v}

form an open cover of

Y
,
such a gluing datum is compatible with further restrictions.
Hence

\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Pi_{\Sigma})

is also the limit of the diagram

\displaystyle\prod_{v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Pi_{\Sigma})}\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{v},y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})\rightarrow\prod_{e\in\operatorname{Edge}(\Pi_{\Sigma})}\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{v}|_{\{y_{e}\neq 0\}},y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})\rightarrow\cdots.

Since the sheaf

\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}

on

Y

satisfies Zariski descent

[
Pre
, Proposition A.3.1]
,
it coincides with

\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y,W_{Y})

and
the compatible equivalences on pieces glue to yield a canonical equivalence

\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Pi_{\Sigma})=\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y,W_{Y})
.
By the same argument,
we obtain a canonical equivalence

\mathcal{F}_{B}(U)=\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{U},W_{Y})

for every open subset

U\subset\Pi_{\Sigma}
.
Since

U_{v}

for all

v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Pi_{\Sigma})

form a subbase,
these equivalences respect restrictions.
∎

5.
Isomorphism of the constructible sheaves

In this section,
we give a proof of HMS for very affine hypersurfaces by gluing HMS for pairs of pants established in

[
Nad
]
.
When gluing such equivalences,
the combinatorial duality over

\Pi_{\Sigma}

from Section

3

plays a crucial role.

5.1.
Local equivalences

Passing to the category of matrix factorizations might be delicate because of Knörrer periodicity.
First,
we show how to lift Nadler’s equivalence,
i.e.,
the equivalence from Corollary

2.31

to the category of matrix factorizations preserving the compatibility with restrictions.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let

[H_{U}]

be a Weinstein subdomain of

[H]
.
Then the skeleton

\operatorname{Core}(H_{U})

of

H_{U}

is a closed subset of the skeleton

\operatorname{Core}(H)

of

H
.

Proof.

Replacing

[H_{U}]

with its radial deformation if necessary,
we may assume that

\operatorname{Core}(H_{U})

is disjoint from

\partial[H_{U}]=\partial_{\infty}H_{U}
.
Indeed,
skeleta of Liouville manifolds of finite type are maximal compact subsets conic with respect to Liouville vector fields.
Since any point in

\partial_{\infty}H_{U}

escapes to infinity along the Liouville vector flow,
it never contributes to

\operatorname{Core}(H_{U})
.
We may assume further that

\operatorname{Core}(H)

is contained in

[H]
.
Then

\operatorname{Core}(H)\cap([H]\setminus[H_{U}]^{\circ})

is an open subset of

\operatorname{Core}(H)
.
Since,
up to deformation,

\operatorname{Core}(H_{U})

coincides with the complement of

\operatorname{Core}(H)\cap([H]\setminus[H_{U}]^{\circ})

in

\operatorname{Core}(H)
,
it is a closed subset of

\operatorname{Core}(H)
.
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

For each vertex

v\in\Pi_{\Sigma}

there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{A}(U_{v})\simeq\operatorname{IndCoh}(Y_{v,d})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}},\ Y_{v,d}=\{y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+1}}=0\}\subset\mathbb{A}^{d+1}

compatible with restrictions.
In particular,
for every open subset

U_{S^{(k)}}\subset U_{v}

determined by edges

e^{v}_{i_{1}},\ldots,e^{v}_{i_{k}}

adjacent to

v
,
we have the commutative diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 37.36685pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.74437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{A}(U_{v})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.24565pt\raise 8.26689pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.1676pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R^{A}_{v,S^{(k)}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 101.4345pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.25002pt\raise-15.97223pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern Recall that

H_{S^{(k)}}

is isomorphic to the intersection of legs

L_{d,i_{1}}(K),\ldots,L_{d,i_{k}}(K)\subset\tilde{P}_{d}
.
We write

\Lambda_{S^{(k)}}

for the isomorphic image of

\operatorname{Core}(H_{S^{(k)}})\times\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}

under the symplectomorphism

U_{d}\times T^{1}\times\mathbb{R}\hookrightarrow\Omega_{d+1}

from
Lemma
2.10
.
Let

\tilde{P}_{I}\subset T^{*}T^{I}

be the tailored pants for

I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\}
.
We write

\mathfrak{L}_{I}

for the isomorphic image of

\operatorname{Core}(\tilde{P}_{I})

under the symplectomorphism from
Lemma
2.10
.
Unwinding the proof of
Lemma
2.7

and
Lemma
2.10
,
one sees that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Core}(H_{S^{(k)}})\cong T^{I^{c}}\times\operatorname{Core}(\tilde{P}_{I})\cong\mathbb{T}^{I^{c}}\times\mathfrak{L}_{I}\cong T^{I^{c}}\times s(\Gamma_{\mathfrak{L}_{I},-f|_{\mathfrak{L}_{I}}})\cong T^{I^{c}}\times\Lambda^{\infty}_{I}\subset\Lambda^{\infty}_{d+1}

and

\Lambda^{\infty}_{S^{(k)}}\subset\Lambda^{\infty}_{d+1}

is defined as

\bigcap^{k}_{j=1}\{\xi_{i_{j}}=0\}
.
Hence we obtain

\displaystyle\bigcup^{k}_{j=1}\Lambda^{\infty}_{\{i_{j}\}^{c}}=\Lambda^{\infty}_{d+1}\setminus\Lambda^{\infty}_{S^{(k)}},\ \operatorname{\mu Sh}(\bigcup^{k}_{j=1}\Lambda^{\infty}_{\{i_{j}\}^{c}})=\operatorname{\mu Sh}(\Lambda^{\infty}_{d+1}\setminus\Lambda^{\infty}_{S^{(k)}}).

Remark 5.3
.

Recall that
Nadler broke the symmetry of

\tilde{P}_{d}

so that
the final leg attracts the Liouville vector flow
while remaining the other legs symmetric.
In particular,
each of the other legs defines a Weinstein submanifold,
which is isomorphic to a product of

\mathbb{C}^{*}

and
a
1
-dimensional lower tailored pants.
Via the combinatorial duality incorporated into the definition of

\mathcal{F}_{B}
,
the Viterbo restriction to such a Weinstein submanifold
associated with the
j
-th leg
corresponds to
the restriction to the open subset defined by

y_{e^{v}_{j}}\neq 0

for

j=1,\ldots,d+1
.

Consider the natural projection

\displaystyle Y_{v,d+1}=\{y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+1}}y_{e^{v}_{d+2}}=0\}\subset Y_{v}\cong\mathbb{A}^{d+2}\to Y^{i}_{v,d+1}=\{y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots\widehat{y_{e^{v}_{i}}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}}=0\}\subset\mathbb{A}^{d+1}

of the union of the coordinate hyperplanes.
By
Lemma
2.28

the pullback induces an equivalence

(5.13)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}(Y^{i}_{v,d+1})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\simeq\operatorname{MF}(\mathbb{A}^{d+2},y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+1}}y_{e^{v}_{d+2}}).

Theorem 5.4
.

For each vertex

v\in\Pi_{\Sigma}

there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{A}(U_{v})\simeq\mathcal{F}_{B}(U_{v})

compatible with restrictions.
In particular,
for every open subset

U_{S^{(k)}}\subset U_{v}

determined by edges

e^{v}_{i_{1}},\ldots,e^{v}_{i_{k}}

adjacent to

v
,
we have the commutative diagram

Proof.

On each vertex

v\in\Pi_{\Sigma}

we have

d+2

Nadler’s equivalences

\displaystyle\varphi^{v}_{i}\colon\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{v})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\to\operatorname{IndCoh}(Y^{i}_{v,d+1})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\to\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(y_{e^{v}_{1}}\ldots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})

depending on the choice of the final leg.
Here,
we use the symbol

\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(y_{e^{v}_{1}}\ldots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})

to denote

\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(\mathbb{A}^{d+2},y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+1}}y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})

for brevity.
The equivalence

\varphi^{v}_{d+2}

is compatible with restrictions along edges except

e^{v}_{d+2}
.
Passing to

\varphi^{v}_{i}

for

i=1,\ldots,d+1
,
we obtain restrictions

\displaystyle\psi^{v}_{i}\circ\varphi^{v}_{i}|_{e^{v}_{d+2}}\colon\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{e^{v}_{d+2}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\to\operatorname{IndCoh}(Y^{i}_{v,d+1}|_{\{y_{e^{v}_{d+2}}\neq 0\}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\to\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}}|_{\{y_{e^{v}_{d+2}}\neq 0\}})

along

e^{v}_{d+2}
,
where

\psi^{v}_{i}

are the autoequivalences

\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})\to\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})

induced by shuffling the coordinates corresponding to the final legs.
By
Lemma
4.3

and
the argument in the proof of
Theorem
2.30

the additional restrictions

\psi^{v}_{i}\circ\varphi^{v}_{i}|_{e^{v}_{d+2}}

do not depend on the choice of

i
.
Namely,
we have the commutative diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.2.
Gluing equivalences

Finally,
we glue the local equivalence from
Theorem
5.4

on each vertex

v\in\Pi_{\Sigma}

to obtain a global equivalence
which is compatible with restrictions.

.

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{A}(\Pi_{\Sigma})\simeq\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Pi_{\Sigma})

.

0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-50.74161pt\raise-33.9245pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0\}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 285.00284pt\raise-50.75035pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.71446pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi^{vv^{\prime}}_{ii^{\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 285.00284pt\raise-59.5762pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\varphi^{v}_{k(v)}|_{e^{v}_{i}}\colon\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{e^{v}_{i}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.06538pt\raise-67.5762pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.44649pt\raise-67.5762pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 77.44649pt\raise-67.5762pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{IndCoh}(Y^{k(v)}_{v,d+1}|_{\{y_{e^{v}_{i}}\neq 0\}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern
