226.71129pt\raise-67.5762pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 226.71129pt\raise-67.5762pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(y_{e^{v}_{1}}\ldots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}}|_{\{y_{e^{v}_{i}}\neq 0\}})}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-42.20512pt\raise-100.95511pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\varphi^{v}_{k(v)}\colon\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{U_{v}})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.57487pt\raise-100.95511pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.94464pt\raise-100.95511pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-84.26566pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.81914pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R^{A}_{v,e^{v}_{i}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-76.63176pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 94.94464pt\raise-100.95511pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{IndCoh}(Y^{k(v)}_{v,d+1})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 209.00641pt\raise-100.95511pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 244.20944pt\raise-100.95511pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 244.20944pt\raise-100.95511pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(y_{e^{v}_{1}}\cdots y_{e^{v}_{d+2}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 285.00284pt\raise-84.26566pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.81914pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R^{B}_{v,e^{v}_{i}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 285.00284pt\raise-79.92796pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

where

\phi^{vv^{\prime}}_{ii^{\prime}}

is the canonical equivalence induced by
(
5.14
).
Clearly,
it yields the commutative diagrams for further restrictions.

Suppose further that

v^{\prime}

is connected with another vertex

v^{\prime\prime}

by an edge

e^{\prime}
.
Then

\Sigma_{Y}

gives the correspondences

(5.23)

\displaystyle y_{e^{v^{\prime}}_{i^{\prime}}}\leftrightarrow y_{e^{v^{\prime\prime}}_{i^{\prime\prime}}},\ i^{\prime}=1,\ldots,d+2

of the coordinates on the intersection

Y_{e^{\prime}}=Y_{v^{\prime}}\cap Y_{v^{\prime\prime}}
.
Hence there is a unique integer

1\leq k(v^{\prime\prime})\leq d+2

such that

\varphi^{v^{\prime\prime}}_{k(v^{\prime\prime})}

is compatible with

\varphi^{v^{\prime}}_{k(v^{\prime})}
.
Namely,
we have the same commutative diagram as
(
5.2
).
On the intersection

Y_{v}\cap Y_{v^{\prime}}\cap Y_{v^{\prime\prime}}
,
the fan

\Sigma_{Y}

also gives the correspondences

(5.24)

\displaystyle y_{e^{v}_{i}}\leftrightarrow y_{e^{v^{\prime\prime}}_{i^{\prime\prime\prime}}},\ i=1,\ldots,d+2

of the coordinates.
Hence there is a unique integer

1\leq k^{\prime}(v^{\prime\prime})\leq d+2

such that

\varphi^{v^{\prime\prime}}_{k^{\prime}(v^{\prime\prime})}

is compatible with

\varphi^{v}_{k(v)}
.
Since the affine pieces

Y_{v},Y_{v^{\prime}},Y_{v^{\prime\prime}}

glue to yield an open subset of

Y
,
the correspondences
(
5.24
)
are compatible with
(
5.14
)
and
(
5.23
).
Namely,
we have

k(v^{\prime\prime})=k^{\prime}(v^{\prime\prime})

and

y_{e^{v^{\prime\prime}}_{i^{\prime\prime}}}=y_{e^{v^{\prime\prime}}_{i^{\prime\prime\prime}}}

for

i=1,\ldots,d+2
.
Hence Nadler’s equivalences

\varphi^{v}_{k(v)},\varphi^{v^{\prime}}_{k(v^{\prime})},\varphi^{v^{\prime\prime}}_{k(v^{\prime\prime})}

glue to yield an equivalence

\displaystyle\varphi^{v}_{k(v)}\cup\varphi^{v^{\prime}}_{k(v^{\prime})}\cup\varphi^{v^{\prime\prime}}_{k(v^{\prime\prime})}\colon\operatorname{Fuk}(H_{v}\cup H_{v^{\prime}}\cup H_{v^{\prime\prime}})\to\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(Y_{v}\cup Y_{v^{\prime}}\cup Y_{v^{\prime\prime}},W_{Y}).

Iteratively,
we obtain a compatible system

\{\varphi^{v}_{k(v)}\}_{v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Pi_{\Sigma})}

of Nadler’s equivalences
whose gluing

\displaystyle\bigcup_{v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Pi_{\Sigma})}\varphi^{v}_{k(v)}\colon\mathcal{F}_{A}(\Pi_{\Sigma})\to\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Pi_{\Sigma})

gives the desired equivalence.
∎

6.
Critical loci of Landau–Ginzburg models for complete intersections

In this section,
following

[
AAK16
, Section 10]
,
we realize the mirror pair for a complete intersection of very affine hypersufaces as critical loci of associated Landau–Ginzburg models.
They give rise to fibrations over the complete intersection of the tropical hypersurfaces
equipped with the canonical stratification.

6.1.
Landau–Ginzburg
A
-model for complete intersections

Let

H_{1},\ldots,H_{r}\subset\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}

be very affine hypersurfaces in general position defined by Laurent polynomials

(6.1)

\displaystyle W_{i}\colon\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\to\mathbb{C},\ x\mapsto\sum_{\alpha^{i}\in A_{i}}c_{\alpha^{i}}t^{-\rho_{i}(\alpha^{i})}x^{\alpha^{i}},\ i=1,\ldots,r.

Here,

•

c_{\alpha^{i}}\in\mathbb{C}^{*}

are arbitrary constants,

•

t\gg 0

is a sufficiently general tropical parameter,

•

\rho_{i}

are convex piecewise linear functions on convex lattice polytopes

\Delta^{\vee}_{i}

whose corner loci give adapted unimodular triangulations

\mathcal{T}_{i}

of

\Delta^{\vee}_{i}
,
and

•

A_{i}\subset M^{\vee}

are the set of vertices of

\mathcal{T}_{i}
.

We denote by

\mathbf{H}

the complete intersection

H_{1}\cap\cdots\cap H_{r}\subset\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}
.
For

\mathbf{X}=\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\times\mathbb{C}^{r}

with coordinates

(x,u)=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d+1},u_{1},\ldots,u_{r})
,
consider a Laurent polynomial

\displaystyle W_{\mathbf{X}}\colon\mathbf{X}\to\mathbb{C},\ (x,u)\mapsto\sum^{r}_{i=1}u_{i}W_{i}(x).

Definition 6.1
.

Let

\mathbf{H}\subset\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}

be the complete intersection of the very affine hypersurfaces

H_{1},\ldots,H_{r}

defined by the Laurent polynomials

W_{1},\ldots,W_{r}

from
(
6.1
).
We call the pair

(\mathbf{X},W_{\mathbf{X}})

the

Landau–Ginzburg
A
-model

for

\mathbf{H}
.

Definition 6.2
.

The

Newton polytope

\Delta^{\vee}_{\mathbf{X}}

of

W_{\mathbf{X}}

is the convex hull

\displaystyle\operatorname{Conv}(0,-\Delta^{\vee}_{1}\times e_{1},\ldots,-\Delta^{\vee}_{r}\times e_{r})\subset M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{r}

where

e_{1},\ldots,e_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}

are the standard basis vectors.

Remark 6.3
.

The polytope

\Delta^{\vee}_{\mathbf{X}}

admits an adapted star-shaped triangulation

\bf{T}

canonically induced by

\rho_{1},\ldots,\rho_{r}
.
However,
it might not be unimodular.

Lemma 6.4
.

The critical locus

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{X}})

is given by

\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{u_{i}=0\}\cap\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{W_{i}=0\}\subset\mathbf{X}
.

Proof.

Express the tangent map

dW_{\mathbf{X}}

of

W_{\mathbf{X}}

as a
(1,2r)
-matrix

\displaystyle(u_{1}dW_{1},\ldots,u_{r}dW_{r},W_{1},\ldots,W_{r}).

Since

H_{1},\ldots,H_{r}\subset\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}

are in general position,
we may assume that
they intersect transversely.
Then

dW_{i}

nowhere vanish.
Hence

\operatorname{rank}(dW_{\mathbf{X}})=0

if and only if

u_{1}=\cdots=u_{r}=0

and

W_{1}=\cdots=W_{r}=0
.
∎

Remark 6.5
.

By
Lemma
6.4

the projection

\operatorname{pr}_{1}\colon\mathbf{X}=\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\times\mathbb{C}^{r}\to\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}

preserves

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{X}})

and
the inclusions

\mathbf{H}\subset H_{i}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{X}})
.
Now,
we may assume that
the tropical hypersurfaces

\Pi_{\Sigma_{1}},\ldots,\Pi_{\Sigma_{r}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d+1}

intersect transversely,
as

H_{1},\ldots,H_{r}\subset\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}

are in general position.
We may assume further that

H_{1},\ldots,H_{r}

intersect along their legs.
Let

\operatorname{ret}_{i}\colon\Pi_{i}\to\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}

be the continuous maps induced by the retractions.
Then the composition

(6.2)

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}\colon\mathbf{H}\cong\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{X}})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{X}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Log}_{d+1}}\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\Pi_{i}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{ret}_{r}\circ\cdots\circ\operatorname{ret}_{1}}\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}

gives a fibration.
Away from lower dimensional strata,
the fiber over a point in the intersection of

r

top dimensional strata one from each

\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}

is a real
(d-r+1)
-torus.

6.2.
Landau–Ginzburg
B
-model for complete intersections

Recall that

\mathcal{T}_{i}

are the chosen adapted unimodular triangulations of

\Delta^{\vee}_{i}\subset M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}

obtained as the corner loci of the convex piecewise linear functions

\rho_{i}\colon\Delta^{\vee}_{i}\to\mathbb{R}
.
Recall also from Remark

6.3

that

\mathbf{T}

is the adapted star-shaped triangulation of

\Delta^{\vee}_{\mathbf{X}}\subset M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{r}

canonically induced by

\rho_{1},\ldots,\rho_{r}
.
Let

\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}\subset M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{r}

be the fan corresponding to

\mathbf{T}

and

\mathbf{Y}

the noncompact
(d+r+1)
-dimensional toric variety associated with

\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}
.
The primitive ray generators of

\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}

are the vectors of the form

(-\alpha^{i,j},e_{i})

with

\alpha^{i,j}\in A_{i}
.
Such vectors span a smooth cone of

\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}

if

-\alpha^{i,j}

span a cell of

\mathcal{T}_{i}

for fixed

i
.

Remark 6.6
.

Unlike the case

r=1
,
there might be nonsmooth cones in

\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}

as

\mathbf{T}

is not necessarily unimodular.
Indeed,
consider the case
where

d=2,r=2

and
the two defining polynomials are

x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3},x^{2}_{1}x_{2}+x_{3}
.
Then the Newton polytope

\Delta^{\vee}_{\mathbf{X}}

is a
5
-dimensional simplex
which has twice volume of the unit simplex.
As

x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3},x^{2}_{1}x_{2}+x_{3}

cannot be further divided,
there is no room for subdivision of

\Delta^{\vee}_{\mathbf{X}}
.
Possible nonsmooth cones would contain at least two rays belonging to distinct subfans of the form

\displaystyle\Sigma_{i}=\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\cdot(-\mathcal{T}_{i}\times\{e_{i}\})\subset\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}.

Dually,

\mathbf{Y}

is associated with the noncompact moment polytope

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbf{Y}}=\{(m,u_{1},\ldots,u_{r})\in M_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{r}\ |\ u_{i}\geq\varphi_{i}(m),\ 1\leq i\leq r\}.

The facets of

\Delta_{\mathbf{Y}}

correspond to the maximal domains of linearity of

\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{r}
.
We denote by

\mathbf{A}

the set of connected components of

\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\setminus\bigcup^{r}_{i=1}\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}

and
index each component by the tuple

\displaystyle\vec{\alpha}=(\alpha^{1,j_{1}},\ldots,\alpha^{r,j_{r}})\in M^{\vee}\times\cdots\times M^{\vee}

of vertices.

Remark 6.7
.

The noncompact polytope

\Delta_{\mathbf{Y}}

is homeomorphic to the image of

\mathbf{Y}

under the composition

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}\to(\mathbf{Y})_{\geq 0}\to M_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{r}

of
the map induced by retraction to the nonnegative real points
with
the restriction of the negated algebraic moment map.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let

\mathbf{q}\colon M_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{r}\to M_{\mathbb{R}}

be the natural projection.
Then under

\mathbf{q}

the union of intersections of

r

facets of

\Delta_{\mathbf{Y}}

one from each

\{u_{i}\geq\varphi_{i}(m)\}

homeomorphically maps to

M_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Moreover,
the union of intersections of

r

codimension

2

faces of

\Delta_{\mathbf{Y}}

one from each

\{u_{i}\geq\varphi_{i}(m)\}

homeomorphically maps to

\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}
.

Proof.

By construction of

\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}

under

\mathbf{q}

the intersection of

r

facets of

\Delta_{\mathbf{Y}}

one from each

\{u_{i}\geq\varphi_{i}(m)\}

homeomorphically maps to the intersection of

r

maximal domains of linearity
one from each

\varphi_{i}

corresponding to the same

\alpha^{i,j_{i}}\in A_{i}
.
When

\vec{\alpha}=(\alpha^{1,j_{1}},\ldots,\alpha^{r,j_{r}})

runs through

\mathbf{A}
,
the closure of the latter covers

M_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Then the second statement follows from the same argument as in the proof of
Lemma
3.12
.
∎

For each

\vec{\alpha}\in\mathbf{A}

let

\mathbf{Y}_{\vec{\alpha}}=\mathbb{T}_{\mathbb{C}}\times\mathbb{C}^{r}

with coordinates

y_{\vec{\alpha}}=(y_{\vec{\alpha}_{,}1},\ldots,y_{\vec{\alpha},d+1},v_{\vec{\alpha},1},\ldots,v_{\vec{\alpha},r})
,
where

y_{\vec{\alpha}_{,}1},\ldots,y_{\vec{\alpha},d+1}

are the monomials with weights

\displaystyle\eta_{1}=(-1,\ldots,0,-\alpha^{1,j_{1}}_{1},\ldots,-\alpha^{r,j_{r}}_{1}),\ldots,\eta_{d+1}=(0,\ldots,-1,-\alpha^{1,j_{1}}_{d+1},\ldots,-\alpha^{r,j_{r}}_{d+1})\in M\times\mathbb{Z}^{r}

and

v_{\vec{\alpha},1},\ldots,v_{\vec{\alpha},r}

are the monomials with weights

\displaystyle\eta_{d+2}=(0,\ldots,0,1,\ldots,0),\ldots,\eta_{d+r+1}=(0,\ldots,0,0,\ldots,1)\in M\times\mathbb{Z}^{r}.

Pairing of the former monomials with the monomials with weight

\displaystyle u_{\xi_{1}}=(-\alpha^{1,j_{1}},e_{1}),\ldots,u_{\xi_{r}}=(-\alpha^{r,j_{r}},e_{r})\in M^{\vee}\times\mathbb{Z}^{r}

yield

0

while that of the latter monomials yield

1
.

Lemma 6.9
.

The complex algebraic variety

\mathbf{Y}_{\vec{\alpha}}

is the affine open subset of

\mathbf{Y}

associated with the cone spanned by

u_{\xi_{1}},\ldots,u_{\xi_{r}}\in M^{\vee}\times\mathbb{Z}^{r}
.

Proof.

Suppose that

\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}(r)

is the cone associated with the affine open subset

\mathbf{Y}_{\vec{\alpha}}\subset\mathbf{Y}
.
We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}(y^{\pm 1}_{\vec{\alpha},i})=\sum_{\xi\in\sigma(1)}\langle\pm\eta_{i},u_{\xi}\rangle D_{\xi},\ \operatorname{div}(v_{\vec{\alpha},j})=\sum_{\xi\in\sigma(1)}\langle\eta_{d+j+1},u_{\xi}\rangle D_{\xi}

for

1\leq i\leq d+1

and

1\leq j\leq r
,
where

u_{\xi}

are the primitive ray generators of

\xi

and

D_{\xi}=\overline{O(\xi)}

are the closures of the orbits corresponding to

\xi
.
Since

y^{\pm 1}_{\vec{\alpha},1},\ldots,y^{\pm 1}_{\vec{\alpha},d+1}

never vanish on

\mathbf{Y}_{\vec{\alpha}}
,
pairing of

\eta_{i}

with the the primitive ray generators in

\sigma

must yield

0

for

1\leq i\leq d+1
.
On the other hand,
pairing of

\eta_{d+j+1}

with

u_{\xi_{k}}

must yield

\delta_{jk}

for

1\leq j,k\leq d+1
.
∎

Due to the above lemma,

\mathbf{Y}_{\vec{\alpha}}

covers the open stratum of

\mathbf{Y}

and
the open strata of the irreducible toric divisors corresponding to

\alpha^{1,j_{1}},\ldots,\alpha^{r,j_{r}}
.
If

\alpha^{i,j_{i}},\beta^{i,k_{i}}

are connected by an edge in

\mathcal{T}_{i}

for some

1\leq i\leq d+1
,
then we glue

\mathbf{Y}_{\vec{\alpha}}

to

\mathbf{Y}_{\vec{\beta}}

with the coordinate transformations

\displaystyle y_{\vec{\alpha},l}=v^{\beta^{i,k_{i}}_{l}-\alpha^{i,j_{i}}_{l}}_{\vec{\beta},i}y_{\vec{\beta},l},\ v_{\vec{\alpha},i}=v_{\vec{\beta},i},\ 1\leq l\leq d+1.

Thus the coordinate charts

\{\mathbf{Y}_{\vec{\alpha}}\}_{\vec{\alpha}\in\mathbf{A}}

cover the complement in

\mathbf{Y}

of the codimension more than

1

strata.

We may write

v_{i}

for

v_{\vec{\alpha},i}

as they do not depend on the choice of

\vec{\alpha}\in\mathbf{A}
.
Since the weights

\displaystyle(0,\ldots,0,1,\ldots,0),\ldots,(0,\ldots,0,0,\ldots,1)\in M\times\mathbb{Z}^{r}

pair nonnegatively with the primitive ray generators of

\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}
,
the polynomial

v_{1}+\cdots+v_{r}

defines a regular function on

\mathbf{Y}
,
which we denote by

W_{\mathbf{Y}}
.

Definition 6.10
.

Let

\mathbf{H}\subset\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}

be the complete intersection of very affine hypersurfaces

H_{1},\ldots,H_{r}

defined by the Laurent polynomials

W_{1},\ldots,W_{r}

from
(
6.1
).
We call the pair

(\mathbf{Y},W_{\mathbf{Y}})

the

Landau–Ginzburg
B
-model

for

\mathbf{H}
.

Remark 6.11
.

The pair

(\mathbf{Y},W_{\mathbf{Y}})

is a conjectural SYZ mirror to

\mathbf{H}

[
AAK16
, Theorem 1.6]
.

Lemma 6.12
.

The critical locus

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{Y}})

is given by

\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\operatorname{Crit}(v_{i})
.

Proof.

Since we have

\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\operatorname{Crit}(v_{i})\subset\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{Y}})
,
it remains to show the opposite inclusion.
For

y\in\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{Y}})

there are

r

rays

\xi_{i,j_{i}}=\operatorname{Cone}(-\alpha^{i,j_{i}}\times e_{i})\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}

one from each

\Sigma_{i}

such that

y\in\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}D_{\xi_{i,j_{i}}}

where

D_{\xi_{i,j_{i}}}=\overline{O(\xi_{i,j_{i}})}

are the closures of the orbits corresponding to

\xi_{i,j_{i}}
.
Indeed,
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{Y}})\subset\mathbf{Y}\setminus(\mathbb{T}_{\mathbb{C}}\times(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{r})=\bigcup_{\xi\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}(1)}D_{\xi}=\bigcup^{r}_{i=1}\bigcup_{\xi_{i}\in\Sigma_{i}(1)}D_{\xi_{i}}.

If

y\notin\bigcup_{\xi_{j}\in\Sigma_{j}(1)}D_{\xi_{j}}

for some

1\leq j\leq r
,
then there is a neighborhood

y\in U\subset\mathbf{Y}

such that

U\cap\bigcup_{\xi_{j}\in\Sigma_{j}(1)}D_{\xi_{j}}=\emptyset

and

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{Y}}|_{U})=\emptyset
,
as

v_{j}

never vanishes on

U
.
Hence

y

belongs to at least one

D_{\xi_{i,j_{i}}}

for each

i

and
we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{Y}})\subset\bigcup_{\vec{\alpha}\in\mathbf{A}}\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}D_{\xi_{i,j_{i}}}.

From the proof of
Lemma
6.8

it follows that
under

\mathbf{q}

the intersection of

r

facets of

\Delta_{\mathbf{Y}}

corresponding to

D_{\xi_{1,j_{1}}},\ldots,D_{\xi_{r,j_{r}}}

maps to the closure

\bar{C}_{\vec{\alpha}}

of the connected component

C_{\vec{\alpha}}\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}\setminus\bigcup^{r}_{i=1}\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}

indexed by

\vec{\alpha}=(\alpha^{1,j_{1}},\ldots,\alpha^{r,j_{r}})
.
By
Lemma
6.9

under
(
6.3
)

\mathbf{Y}_{\vec{\alpha}}

maps to the intersection of

r

facets of

\Delta_{\mathbf{Y}}

corresponding to

D_{\xi_{1,j_{1}}},\ldots,D_{\xi_{r,j_{r}}}
.
As explained above,
on

\mathbf{Y}_{\vec{\alpha}}

we are given the coordinates

\displaystyle(w^{-1}_{1}v^{-\alpha^{1,j_{1}}_{1}}_{1}\cdots v^{-\alpha^{r,j_{r}}_{1}}_{r},\ldots,w^{-1}_{r}v^{-\alpha^{1,j_{1}}_{d+1}}_{1}\cdots v^{-\alpha^{r,j_{r}}_{d+1}}_{r},v_{1},\ldots,v_{r})

which implies

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{Y}})|_{\mathbf{Y}_{\vec{\alpha}}}=\emptyset
.
Hence we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{Y}})\subset\bigcup_{\vec{\alpha}\in\mathbf{A}}\left(\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}D_{\xi_{i,j_{i}}}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{\vec{\alpha}}\right)=\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\operatorname{Crit}(v_{i})

as we have

\bigcup_{\alpha^{i,j_{i}}\in A_{i}}(D_{\xi_{i,j_{i}}}\setminus(Y_{\alpha^{i}}\times\mathbb{C}^{r-1}))=\operatorname{Crit}(v_{i})

by
Lemma
3.16
.
∎

Remark 6.13
.

Since the map
(
6.3
)
sends each
k
-th intersection of

D_{\xi_{i,j_{i}}},\alpha^{i,j_{i}}\in\mathbf{A}

to a codimension

k

face of

\Delta_{\mathbf{Y}}
,
by
Lemma
6.12

it sends

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{Y}})

to the union of codimension

2r

faces.
On the other hand,
by
Lemma
6.8

the map

\mathbf{q}\colon M_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{r}\to M_{\mathbb{R}}

homeomorphically sends the union of intersections of

r

codimension

2

faces of

\Delta_{\mathbf{Y}}

one from each

\{u_{i}\geq\varphi_{i}(m)\}

to

\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}
.
Hence the composition

(6.4)

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}\colon\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{Y}})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{Y}\xrightarrow{\textup{(\ref{eq:cmoment})}}\Delta_{\mathbf{Y}}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{q}}\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}

gives a fibration.
The fiber over a point in the intersection of

r

top dimensional strata
one from each

\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}

is a real
(d-r+1)
-torus

[
CLS11
, Prop 12.2.3(b)]
.

7.
Equivariantization and de-equivariantization

In this section,
following

[
She22
, Section 4]
,
we review the last piece of our proof,
i.e.,
equivariantization
and
de-equivaiantization
of presentable dg categories with certain group actions.
The fact that,
they give mutually inverse equivalences of the categories
we will consider,
enables us to deduce our main result for nonunimodular case from unimodular case.

7.1.
Equivariantization

Let

G\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{N}

be any subgroup.
Assume that

G

acts on a presentable dg category

\mathscr{C}
.
Namely,
there is a monoidal functor

G\to\operatorname{End}(\mathscr{C})
.
Then

\mathscr{C}

becomes a module over the monoidal category

(\operatorname{Qcoh}(G),\star)
,
where

\star

is the convolution product induced by the multiplication on

G
.
Let

(\operatorname{Qcoh}(BG),\otimes)

be the monoidal category of
G
-representations.
Taking
G
-invariants defines a functor

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}\mapsto\mathscr{C}^{G}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}(G)}(\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbb{C}),\mathscr{C})

from the category of

(\operatorname{Qcoh}(G),\star)
-modules
to the category of

(\operatorname{Qcoh}(BG),\otimes)
-modules,
called

G
-equivariantization
.
Here,
the action on

\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbb{C})

is trivial.

7.2.
De-equivariantization

We denote by

G^{\vee}

the character group

\operatorname{Hom}(G,\mathbb{C}^{*})

of

G
.
Assume that

G^{\vee}

acts on a presentable dg category

\mathscr{D}
.
This is the same as an action of the monoidal category of
G^{\vee}
-graded
\mathbb{C}
-modules,
which in turn is equivalent to

(\operatorname{Qcoh}(BG),\otimes)
.
Hence

\mathscr{D}

becomes a module over

(\operatorname{Qcoh}(BG),\otimes)
.
Taking
G
-coinvariants defines a functor

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}\mapsto\mathscr{D}_{BG}=\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbb{C})\otimes_{\operatorname{Qcoh}(BG)}\mathscr{D}

from the category of

(\operatorname{Qcoh}(BG),\otimes)
-modules
to the category of

(\operatorname{Qcoh}(G),\star)
-modules,
called

G
-de-equivariantization
.
Also here,
the action on

\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbb{C})

is trivial.

7.3.
Mutually inverse equivalences

For a presentable dg category

\mathscr{D}

with a
G^{\vee}
-action,
taking its
G^{\vee}
-invariants
is equivalent to
taking its
G
-coinvariants.
Since
equivariantization
and
de-equivariantization
give mutually inverse equivalences,
one obtains

Lemma 7.1

(
[
She15
, Lemma 8]
)
.

Let

G\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{N}

be any subgroup
and

G^{\vee}=\operatorname{Hom}(G,\mathbb{C}^{*})
.
Then

G
-equivariantization
and

G^{\vee}
-equivariantization
give mutually inverse equivalences between
the category of presentable dg categories with a
G
-action
and
that with a
G^{\vee}
-action.

7.4.
Quotient construction of toric varieties

Let

Y_{\Sigma}

be the toric variety associated with a fan

\Sigma\subset M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Assume that

Y_{\Sigma}

has no torus factors,
i.e.,

M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}

is spanned by primitive ray generators

u_{\rho}

for all

\rho\in\Sigma(1)

[
CLS11
, Proposition 3.3.9]
.
Then

[
CLS11
, Theorem 4.1.3]

gives the short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to M\to\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma(1)}=\bigoplus_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\mathbb{Z}D_{\rho}\to\operatorname{Cl}(Y_{\Sigma})\to 0,

where

m\in M

maps to

\operatorname{div}(\chi^{m})=\Sigma_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\langle m,u_{\rho}\rangle D_{\rho}

and

\operatorname{Cl}(Y_{\Sigma})

is the divisor class group.
Applying

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(-,\mathbb{C}^{*})
,
one obtains another short exact sequence

\displaystyle 1\to G=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\operatorname{Cl}(Y_{\Sigma}),\mathbb{C}^{*})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma(1)},\mathbb{C}^{*})\cong(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{\Sigma(1)}\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(M,\mathbb{C}^{*})\cong M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\to 1.

Lemma 7.2

(
[
CLS11
, Lemma 5.1.1]
)
.

The subgroup

G\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{\Sigma(1)}

is isomorphic to a product of
an algebraic torus
and
a finite abelian group.
More explicitly,
we have

\displaystyle G=\{(t_{\rho})\in(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{\Sigma(1)}|\prod_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}t^{\langle m,u_{\rho}\rangle}_{\rho}=1\ \text{for all}\ m\in M\}.

Let

S=\mathbb{C}[y_{\rho}\ |\ \rho\in\Sigma(1)]

be the total coordinate ring of

Y_{\Sigma}
.
Then we have

\mathbb{C}^{\Sigma(1)}=\operatorname{Spec}S

and
the irrelevant ideal is defined as

\displaystyle B(\Sigma)=\langle y^{\hat{\sigma}}\ |\ \sigma\in\Sigma\rangle=\langle y^{\hat{\sigma}}\ |\ \sigma\in\Sigma_{\max}\rangle\subset S,\ y^{\hat{\sigma}}=\prod_{\rho\notin\sigma(1)}y_{\rho}.

We denote by

Z(\Sigma)

the zero locus

V(B(\Sigma))\subset\mathbb{C}^{\Sigma(1)}

of

B(\Sigma)
.
Via inclusion

G\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{\Sigma(1)}

the canonical action of

(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{\Sigma(1)}

on

\mathbb{C}^{\Sigma(1)}

induces a
G
-action on

\mathbb{C}^{\Sigma(1)}\setminus Z(\Sigma)
.

Lemma 7.3

(
[
CLS11
, Proposition 5.1.9, Theorem 5.1.11]
)
.

There is a toric morphism

\displaystyle\pi\colon\mathbb{C}^{\Sigma(1)}\setminus Z(\Sigma)\to Y_{\Sigma}

which is constant on
G
-orbit
and
gives an isomorphism

\displaystyle Y_{\Sigma}\cong(\mathbb{C}^{\Sigma(1)}\setminus Z(\Sigma))//G.

Namely,

\pi

is an almost geometric quotient for the
G
-action.
It is a geometric quotient
if and only if

\Sigma

is simplicial.

Now,
we drop the assumption that

Y_{\Sigma}

has no torus factors.
Then the primitive ray generators

u_{\rho},\rho\in\Sigma(1)

span a proper subspace

(M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}})^{\prime}\subsetneq M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Pick the complement

(M^{\vee})^{\prime\prime}

of

(M^{\vee})^{\prime}=(M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}})^{\prime}\cap M^{\vee}

so that

M^{\vee}=(M^{\vee})^{\prime}\oplus(M^{\vee})^{\prime\prime}
.
The cones of

\Sigma

defines a fan

\Sigma^{\prime}\subset(M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}})^{\prime}
.
Note that
we have

\Sigma^{\prime}(1)=\Sigma(1)

and

B(\Sigma^{\prime})=B(\Sigma)\subset S
.
We denote by

G^{\prime}

the subgroup

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\operatorname{Cl}(Y_{\Sigma^{\prime}}),\mathbb{C}^{*})\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{\Sigma^{\prime}(1)}
.
Since

Y_{\Sigma^{\prime}}

has no torus factors,
from the above argument it follows

\displaystyle Y_{\Sigma}\cong Y_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\times(M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}})^{\prime\prime}\cong((\mathbb{C}^{\Sigma^{\prime}(1)}\setminus Z(\Sigma^{\prime}))//G^{\prime})\times(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{r}.

7.5.
Model case

Let

Y_{\Sigma}

be a simplicial affine toric variety associated with a fan

\Sigma\subset M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Then

Y_{\Sigma}

has no torus factors.
Since

\Sigma

contains only one top dimensional cone,
by definition of

B(\Sigma)

and
Lemma
7.3

we obtain

Z(\Sigma)=\emptyset

and

\displaystyle Y_{\Sigma}\cong\mathbb{A}^{d+r+1}/G,\ d+r+1=\#\Sigma(1).

If also the rank of

M

is

d+r+1
,
then the inclusion

M\to\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma(1)}

induces a finite cover

\displaystyle h^{\vee}\colon T^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d+r+1}/M)\to T^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d+r+1}/\mathbb{Z}^{d+r+1}).

Consider the tailored pants

\tilde{P}_{d+r}

in the target induced by the pants

\displaystyle\{1+\Sigma^{d+r}_{i=1}x_{i}x_{d+r+1}=0\}=\{x^{-1}_{d+r+1}+\Sigma^{d+r}_{i=1}x_{i}=0\}\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+r+1}.

Let

\tilde{P}_{d+r-1}\subset\tilde{P}_{d+r}

be its closed subset induced by setting

x_{d+r+1}=1
.
From

G^{\vee}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{d+r+1}/M

it follows

\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{d+r-1}\cong(h^{\vee})^{-1}(\tilde{P}_{d+r-1})/G^{\vee}.

Hence we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(\tilde{P}_{d+r-1})\simeq\operatorname{Fuk}((h^{\vee})^{-1}(\tilde{P}_{d+r-1}))^{G^{\vee}}\simeq\operatorname{Fuk}((h^{\vee})^{-1}(\tilde{P}_{d+r-1}))_{BG}.

Taking
G
-invariants,
we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(\tilde{P}_{d+r-1})^{G}\simeq(\operatorname{Fuk}((h^{\vee})^{-1}(\tilde{P}_{d+r-1}))_{BG})^{G}\simeq\operatorname{Fuk}((h^{\vee})^{-1}(\tilde{P}_{d+r-1})).

Via HMS for pairs of pants the left most term is equivalent to

\displaystyle\operatorname{MF}(\mathbb{A}^{d+r+1},y_{1}\cdots y_{d+r+1})^{G}\simeq\operatorname{MF}(\mathbb{A}^{d+r+1}/G,y_{1}\cdots y_{d+r+1}).

Note that
by
Lemma
7.2

the product

y_{1}\cdots y_{d+r+1}

is invariant under the
G
-action.
Thus we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{MF}(\mathbb{A}^{d+r+1}/G,y_{1}\cdots y_{d+r+1})\simeq\operatorname{Fuk}((h^{\vee})^{-1}(\tilde{P}_{d+r-1})).

8.
Intersections and categories

As explained in Remark

6.5
,

6.13
,
the critical loci

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{X}}),\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{Y}})

dually project onto

\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}

under

\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}
.
We introduce a topology on

\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}

induced from that on

\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}

defined as in Definition

4.1
.
Then

\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}

admits an open cover by the intersections

\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}S^{(k_{i})}_{i}

of
k_{i}
-strata

S^{(k_{i})}_{i}

of

\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}}
.
In this section,
we establish equivalences of corresponding categories over

\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}S^{(k_{i})}_{i}

and
glue them to yield HMS for complete intersections of very affine hypersurfaces.

8.1.
Covering complete intersections

By
Lemma
6.4

we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{X}})=\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{u_{i}=0\}\cap\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{W_{i}=0\}\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{r},

where

\{W_{i}=0\}

are given by the union

\bigcup_{v^{i}\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Pi_{\Sigma_{i}})}\tilde{P}_{v^{i}}\times\mathbb{C}^{r}
.
We denote by

L_{v^{i}}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i})

the intersection of

k_{i}

legs of

\tilde{P}_{v^{i}}

corresponding to

S^{(k_{i})}_{i}
.
Since by assumption

H_{1},\ldots,H_{r}

are in general position,
we may assume that

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{X}})

is the union

\displaystyle\bigcup_{v^{1},\ldots,v^{r}}\bigcup_{S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r}}\left(\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{u_{i}=0\}\cap\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\left(L_{v^{i}}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i})\times\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)\right).

Under

\mathbf{f}

it projects onto

\bigcup_{S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r}}\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}S^{(k_{i})}_{i}
.

We denote by

\sigma(S^{(k_{i})}_{i})

the cones in the subfans

\Sigma_{i}\subset\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d+r+1}

corresponding to

S^{(k_{i})}_{i}

and
by

\xi_{1}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i}),\ldots,\xi_{d+2-k_{i}}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i})\in\Sigma_{i}(1)

the rays spanning

\sigma(S^{(k_{i})}_{i})
.
Since by assumption

\Pi_{\Sigma_{1}},\ldots,\Pi_{\Sigma_{r}}

intersect transversely,
the rays

(8.1)

\displaystyle\xi_{1}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1}),\ldots,\xi_{d+2-k_{1}}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1}),\ldots,\xi_{1}(S^{(k_{r})}_{r}),\ldots,\xi_{d+2-k_{r}}(S^{(k_{r})}_{r})\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}(1)

span a
(\Sigma^{r}_{i=1}d+2-k_{i})
-dimensional cone

\sigma(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r})\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y}}
.
Note that
we have

\displaystyle\sum^{r}_{i=1}d+2-k_{i}\leq d+r+1.

Under

\mathbf{g}

the union of intersections of

\displaystyle U(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r})=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\sigma^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r})\cap(M\times\mathbb{Z}^{r})]

and

\operatorname{Crit}(W_{\mathbf{Y}})

projects onto

\bigcup_{S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r}}\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}S^{(k_{i})}_{i}
.

8.2.
Local
A
-side categories for complete intersections

Let

\vec{\beta}

be a
\Sigma^{r}_{i=1}l_{i}
-tuple of vertices

\displaystyle\beta^{1,1},\ldots,\beta^{1,l_{1}}\in A_{1},\ldots,\beta^{r,1},\ldots,\beta^{r,l_{r}}\in A_{r}

which together with the origin define rays

\displaystyle\xi_{d+3-k_{1}}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1}),\ldots,\xi_{d+2-k_{1}+l_{1}}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1})\in\Sigma_{1}(1),\ldots,\xi_{d+3-k_{r}}(S^{(k_{r})}_{r}),\ldots,\xi_{d+2-k_{r}+l_{r}}(S^{(k_{r})}_{r})\in\Sigma_{r}(1)

spanning a top dimensional cone

\sigma(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{Y},\max}

together with the rays
(
8.1
).
We denote by

\Delta^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

the
(d+r+1)
-simplex

\displaystyle\operatorname{Conv}(0,(-\alpha^{1,1},e_{1}),\ldots,(-\alpha^{1,d+2-k_{1}+l_{1}},e_{1}),\ldots,(-\alpha^{r,1},e_{r}),\ldots,(-\alpha^{r,d+2-k_{r}+l_{r}},e_{r}))

with

\xi_{j}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i})=\operatorname{Cone}(-\alpha^{i,j},e_{i})

and

\alpha^{i,d+2-k_{i}+j_{i}}=\beta^{i,j_{i}}
.

Recall that
the tailored

\Delta^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})
-pants

\tilde{P}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

is the inverse image of

\tilde{P}_{d+r}

under the map

\displaystyle h^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})\colon(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+r+1}\to(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+r+1},

whose restriction gives a finite cover of

\tilde{P}_{d+r}
.
Here,

h^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

is induced by a homomorphism

\displaystyle h(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d+r+1}\to\mathbb{Z}^{d+r+1}

of lattices
which sends

\Delta^{\vee}_{d+r+1}

to

\Delta^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})
.
Now,
assume that

\Delta^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

is unimodular.
Then

h^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

becomes an isomorphism
and
the monomials

\displaystyle x^{-\alpha^{1,1},e_{1}},\ldots,x^{-\alpha^{1,d+2-k_{1}+l_{1}},e_{1}},\ldots,x^{-\alpha^{r,1},e_{r}},\ldots,x^{-\alpha^{r,d+2-k_{r}+l_{r}},e_{r}}

give coordinates on the target

(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+r+1}
.

Consider the product

\displaystyle\tilde{H}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\vec{\beta})\times\cdots\times\tilde{H}(S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+2-k_{1}+l_{1}}\times\cdots\times(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+2-k_{r}+l_{r}}=(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+r+1}

of very affine hypersurfaces

\displaystyle\tilde{H}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta})=\{\sum^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}_{j_{i}=1}x^{-\alpha^{i,j_{i}},e_{i}}=0\}\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+r+1}.

We denote by

H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta})

the quotients of

\tilde{H}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta})

by the
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u_{i}}
-action

\displaystyle(u_{i},x^{-\alpha^{i,1},e_{i}},\ldots,x^{-\alpha^{i,d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}},e_{i}})\mapsto(u_{i}x^{-\alpha^{i,1},e_{i}},\ldots,u_{i}x^{-\alpha^{i,d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}},e_{i}}),

which are isomorphic to
(d-k_{i}+l_{i})
-dimensional tailored pants up to deformation.
Locally,

\mathbf{H}

is given by a product of

r

lower dimensional tailored pants.

Lemma 8.1
.

Assume that

\Delta^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

is unimodular.
Then the product

\displaystyle H(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\vec{\beta})\times\cdots\times H(S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+1-k_{1}+l_{1}}\times\cdots\times(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+1-k_{r}+l_{r}}=(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+1}

is isomorphic to the intersection

\displaystyle\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{u_{i}=0\}\cap\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{\sum^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}_{j_{i}=1}x^{-\alpha^{i,j_{i}}}=0\}\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{r}.

Proof.

The quotient of

\times^{r}_{i=1}\tilde{H}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta})

by the
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{r}_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{r}}
-action

\displaystyle(u_{1},\ldots,u_{r},x^{-\alpha^{1,1},e_{1}},\ldots,x^{-\alpha^{1,d+2-k_{1}+l_{1}},e_{1}},\ldots,x^{-\alpha^{r,1},e_{r}},\ldots,x^{-\alpha^{r,d+2-k_{r}+l_{r}},e_{r}})

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(u_{1}x^{-\alpha^{1,1},e_{1}},\ldots,u_{1}x^{-\alpha^{1,d+2-k_{1}+l_{1}},e_{1}},\ldots,u_{r}x^{-\alpha^{r,1},e_{r}},\ldots,u_{r}x^{-\alpha^{r,d+2-k_{r}+l_{r}},e_{r}})

is

\times^{r}_{i=1}H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta})
.
Since we have

\displaystyle\times^{r}_{i=1}\tilde{H}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta})=\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\left(\{\sum^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}_{j_{i}=1}x^{-\alpha^{i,j_{i}},e_{i}}=0\}\times(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{r-1+k_{i}-l_{i}}_{\hat{x}^{-\alpha^{i,1},e_{i}},\ldots,\hat{x}^{-\alpha^{i,d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}},e_{i}}}\right),

the quotient is isomorphic to

\displaystyle\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{u_{i}=\epsilon\}\cap\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{\sum^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}_{j_{i}=1}x^{-\alpha^{i,j_{i}},e_{i}}=0\}\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+r+1},

which in turn is isomorphic to

\displaystyle\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{u_{i}=0\}\cap\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{\sum^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}_{j_{i}=1}x^{-\alpha^{i,j_{i}}}=0\}\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{r}.

∎

Corollary 8.2
.

Assume that

\Delta^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

is unimodular.
Then there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{u_{i}=0\}\cap\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{\Sigma^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}_{j_{i}=1}x^{-\alpha^{i,j_{i}}}=0\})\simeq\bigotimes^{r}_{i=1}\operatorname{Fuk}(H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta})).

Proof.

As an open submanifold of a closed submanifold

\mathbf{H}

of

(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+r+1}
,
the intersection

\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{u_{i}=0\}\cap\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{\Sigma^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}_{j_{i}=1}x^{-\alpha^{i,j_{i}}}=0\}

carries a Stein manfold structure,
which in turn defines a Weinstein structure.
Then the claim follows from

[
GPS2
, Theorem 1.5, Corollary 1.18]
.
∎

8.3.
Local
B
-side categories for complete intersections

Also in this subsection,
we assume

\Delta^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

to be unimodular.
Let

\displaystyle y_{1}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1}),\ldots,y_{d+2-k_{1}+l_{1}}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1}),\ldots,y_{1}(S^{(k_{r})}_{r}),\ldots,y_{d+2-k_{r}+l_{r}}(S^{(k_{r})}_{r})

be local coordinates for

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{d+2-k_{1}+l_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{A}^{d+2-k_{r}+l_{r}}

\displaystyle\cong U(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\sigma^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})\cap(M\times\mathbb{Z}^{r})]\subset\mathbf{Y}.

Lemma 8.3
.

Assume that

\Delta^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

is unimodular.
Then there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(U(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta}),W_{\mathbf{Y}})\simeq\bigotimes^{r}_{i=1}\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(\mathbb{A}^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}},y_{1}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i})\cdots y_{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i})).

Proof.

Since we have

\displaystyle W_{\mathbf{Y}}|_{U(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})}=y_{1}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1})\cdots y_{d+2-k_{1}+l_{1}}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1})+\cdots+y_{1}(S^{(k_{r})}_{r})\cdots y_{d+2-k_{r}+l_{r}}(S^{(k_{r})}_{r}),

the claim follows from
Lemma
3.16
,
Lemma
6.12

and

[
Pre
, Theorem 4.1.3]
.
∎

8.4.
Gluing equivalences

Lemma 8.4
.

Assume that

\Delta^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

is unimodular.
Then there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{u_{i}=0\}\cap\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{\Sigma^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}_{j_{i}=1}x^{-\alpha^{i,j_{i}}}=0\})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\simeq\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(U(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta}),W_{\mathbf{Y}}).

Proof.

Due to Corollary

8.2

and
Lemma
8.3

it suffices to show an equivalence

(8.2)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta}))_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\simeq\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(\mathbb{A}^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}},y^{i}_{1}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i})\cdots y^{i}_{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i})).

This follows from
Theorem
5.4
,
since by construction

H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta})

is isomorphic to
(d-k_{i}+l_{i})
-dimensional tailored pants up to deformation.
∎

Theorem 8.5
.

Assume that

\Delta^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

are unimodular for all

S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r}

whose intersection is nonempty.
Then there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(\mathbf{H})_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\simeq\lim_{S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta}}\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(U(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta}),W_{\mathbf{Y}})=\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(\mathbf{Y},W_{\mathbf{Y}})

Proof.

We glue the local equivalences from
Lemma
8.4
.
Again,
due to Corollary

8.2

and
Lemma
8.3

it suffices to show the compatibility of
(
8.2
)
with gluing,
which follows from the same argument as the proof of
Theorem
5.5
.
∎

8.5.
Nonunimodular case

Finally,
we drop the assumption on

\Delta^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

to be unimodular.
The simplicial toric variety

U(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

becomes isomorphic to the geometric quotient

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{d+r+1}/G(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta}).

by the finite abelian subgroup

G(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

of

(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+r+1}

with canonically induced action.
The inclusion extends to a short exact sequence

(8.3)

\displaystyle 0\to G(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})\to(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+r+1}\to M^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})_{\mathbb{C}}\to 0,

where

M^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

is the cocharacter lattice associated with

U(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(\mathbb{A}^{d+r+1}/G(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta}),W_{\mathbf{Y}})\simeq\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(\mathbb{A}^{d+r+1},W_{\mathbf{Y}})^{G(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})}.

On the
A
-side,

\tilde{P}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

becomes a finite cover of

\tilde{P}_{d+r}
.
Since

U(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

has no torus factors,
(
8.3
)
is obtained from the short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to M(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})\to\mathbb{Z}^{d+r+1}\to G^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})\to 0

by taking

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(-,\mathbb{C}^{*})
.
Here,
we use the symbol

G^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

to denote the divisor class group of

U(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})
,
which acts on

\displaystyle(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d+r+1}\cong T^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d+r+1}/M(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})).

Then the finite cover is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{P}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})\to\tilde{P}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})/G^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})=\tilde{P}_{d+r}.

Similarly replace

\displaystyle\times^{r}_{i=1}\tilde{H}(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta})=\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\left(\{\sum^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}_{j_{i}=1}x^{-\alpha^{i,j_{i}},e_{i}}=0\}\times(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{r-1+k_{i}-l_{i}}_{\hat{x}^{-\alpha^{i,1},e_{i}},\ldots,\hat{x}^{-\alpha^{i,d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}},e_{i}}}\right)=\bigcap^{r}_{i=1}\{\sum^{d+2-k_{i}+l_{i}}_{j_{i}=1}x^{-\alpha^{i,j_{i}},e_{i}}=0\}

and

\times^{r}_{i=1}H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta})

with their inverse images.
Then we have

(8.4)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}((\times^{r}_{i=1}H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta}))/G^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta}))\simeq\operatorname{Fuk}(\times^{r}_{i=1}H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta}))^{G^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})}.

As explained above,
there is an equivalence to the de-equivariantization

(8.5)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fuk}(\times^{r}_{i=1}H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta}))^{G^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})}\simeq\operatorname{Fuk}(\times^{r}_{i=1}H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta}))_{BG(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})}

with respect to

G(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})
.
Combining
(
8.4
),
(
8.5
)
and
the equivalence for unimodular case,
we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(\mathbb{A}^{d+r+1},W_{\mathbf{Y}})\simeq(\operatorname{Fuk}(\times^{r}_{i=1}H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta}))_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})_{BG(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})}.

Passing to the equivariantization,
we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(\mathbb{A}^{d+r+1}/G(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta}),W_{\mathbf{Y}})\simeq((\operatorname{Fuk}(\times^{r}_{i=1}H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta}))_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})_{BG(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})})^{G(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})}

which by
Lemma
7.1

implies

(8.6)

\displaystyle\operatorname{MF}^{\infty}(U(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta}),W_{\mathbf{Y}})\simeq\operatorname{Fuk}((\times^{r}_{i=1}H(S^{(k_{i})}_{i},\vec{\beta}))).

We glue the above local equivalences.
It suffices to show the compatibility of
(
8.6
)
with gluing,
which follows from the same argument as the proof of
Theorem
5.5

extended in a straight forward way.
Indeed,
the compatibility of the actions of

G(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

and

G^{\vee}(S^{(k_{1})}_{1},\ldots,S^{(k_{r})}_{r},\vec{\beta})

with gluing follows from
Lemma
7.2

and
the combinatorial duality.
